“I have never uttered that word to any woman before—I had a kind of reverence for it, but then I have never loved a woman before. I was always in love with something particular about them—the corners of Cesca’s mouth, for instance, when she smiled; her unconscious coquetry. There was always one thing or other that took my fancy and inspired me to invent adventures about them—adventures I wanted to experience. I fell in love with one woman because the first time I saw her she wore a beautiful red silk dress, almost black in the folds, like the darkest of roses. I thought of her always in that dress. And with you that time in Viterbo—you were so sweet, so gentle and reserved, and there was a glint in your eyes when the rest of us laughed as if you would have liked to be merry with us, but dared not. That time I was in love with the idea of seeing you gay and smiling.”

“But I have never loved another human being till now.”

===Chinese===
「才不是呢，这种爱谁都给得出。我从前不也深信，自己除了——几段小小的情缘，什么也经历不到吗？说实话，我原本不相信世上还有别的东西存在。」他的声音低了下去。「你是我爱上的第一个女人。」

她站着不动，一言不发。

「这个词，我从来没有对任何女人说过——我对它怀有一种近乎敬畏的心情，不过那时我也从没爱过一个女人。我总是爱上她们身上的某个特别之处——比如切斯卡微笑时嘴角的模样，她那种不自觉的妩媚。总有这样那样一点什么勾动我的心，引得我围绕她们编造出种种奇遇——那是我渴望亲身经历的奇遇。我爱过一个女人，只因为初见她时，她穿一条美丽的红绸裙，裙褶处深得近乎黑色，像颜色最深的一种玫瑰。我想起她时，她永远穿着那条裙子。还有你，维泰博（Viterbo）那一次——你那么甜美，那么温柔而矜持；我们旁人大笑的时候，你眼睛里闪过一点光，仿佛你也想同我们一起快活，却不敢。那一回，我爱上的是这样一个念头：看见你欢畅欢笑的模样。」

「可是直到如今，我才头一次真正爱了一个人。」

===Original===
He turned his face from her, staring at the jet of water rising in the moonlight, and the new sensation in him rose too, inspiring him with new words, which burst in ecstasy from his lips:

“I love you so much, Jenny, that everything else is of no account. I am not sorry you don’t love me in return, for I know you will some day; I feel that my love is strong enough to make you return it. I have time to wait; my happiness will be in loving you.

“When you spoke about being trampled upon and throwing yourself under the train, something happened to me. I could not explain what it was. I knew only that I could not listen to you saying such things. I knew I would never allow it to happen—not for my life. And when you spoke of the child, I felt infinitely sad to think that you had suffered so intensely and that I could not do anything for you. And I was sad too because I wanted you to love me. Everything you said was echoed in my soul—the boundless love and the bitter longing—and I understood that my love for you was just that. While we were in the *trattoria* and walking out here it has grown more and more clear to me how much you are to me, how I love you—and it seems to me that it has always been so. All I know and remember of you is part of it. I understand now why I have been so depressed since you came here. It was because I saw how you suffered. You were so quiet and sad the first weeks, and then came those fits of dissipation—and I remember that day on the road to Warnemünde, when you were crying against my shoulder—everything that concerns you is part of my love for you.

“I know how it all happened with the other men you have known—the boy’s father too. You have talked and talked with them about all you have been thinking, and there was no response to your words even when you tried to make them realize what you felt, because they could not understand your mind. But I know it—all you have been telling me today and what you said to me that day in Warnemünde you could not have told to anybody else. Only to me, because I understand—is it not so?”

===Chinese===
他把脸从她那边转开，望着月光里升起的水柱；他心里那股新的情感也随之升腾，激发出新的话语，从他唇边狂喜地迸发出来：

「我爱你爱得这样深，珍妮，别的一切都不算什么了。你如今不爱我，我并不难过，因为我知道总有一天你会爱的；我感觉得到，我的爱强大得足以让你回应它。我等得起；我的幸福就在爱你本身。

「你说到遭人践踏、要扑到火车底下去的时候，我心里起了一种什么变化。我说不清那是什么。我只知道，听你说那样的话，我受不了。我知道我决不容许那样的事发生——豁出命去也不容许。你说到那孩子的时候，想到你受了那么深的苦，而我什么也帮不上，我觉得无限悲伤。我悲伤，也因为我要你爱我。你说的一切都在我灵魂里激起回响——那没有边际的爱和那苦涩的渴念——我明白了，我对你的爱正是这样的。我们在『小饭馆』里坐着、一路走出来的时候，我心里越来越清楚你对我有多重要，我多么爱你——而且在我看来，事情从来就是如此。我所知道、所记得的关于你的一切，都是这份爱的一部分。我现在明白，你来了以后我为什么那样郁郁寡欢。那是因为我看见你在受苦。头几个星期你那样安静、那样忧伤，接着是那些放浪的发作——我还记得去瓦尔内明德（Warnemünde）路上那一天，你伏在我肩头哭——凡是与你有关的一切，都是我对你的爱的一部分。

「你认识的那些男人——还有那孩子的父亲——这一切是怎么发生的，我都知道。你同他们谈了又谈，把心里想的都说给他们听，可纵然你竭力让他们明白你的感受，你的话也得不到回应，因为他们理解不了你的心。可我知道——你今天对我讲的这一切，还有那天在瓦尔内明德你对我说的话，你是讲给任何别人都讲不出来的。只讲给我，因为我懂——是不是这样？」

===Original===
She bent her head in surprised assent. It was true.

“I know that I alone understand you thoroughly. I know exactly what you are, and I love you as you are. If your mind were full of stains and bleeding wounds, I would love and kiss them until you were clean and well again. My love has no other purpose but to see you become what you always wanted to be and must be to feel happy. If you did ever so foolish a thing, I would only think you were ill or that some strange influence had poisoned your mind. If you deceived me or if I found you lying drunk in the road, you would be my own darling Jenny just the same.

“Will you not be mine—give yourself to me? Will you not come into my arms and let me hold you and make you happy and whole? I don’t know now quite how to set about it, but my love will teach me, and every morning you will wake up less sad—every day will seem a little brighter and warmer than the day before, and your sorrow less great. Let us go to Viterbo or anywhere you like. Give yourself to me, and I will nurse you as if you were a sick child. When you are well again you will have learnt to love me and to know that we two cannot live without each other.

“You are ill; you cannot look after yourself. Close your eyes and give me your hands; I will love you and make you well—I know I can do it.”

===Chinese===
她惊讶地低下头，表示默认。这是真的。

「我知道，只有我一个人彻底懂你。我确切地知道你是什么样的人，我就爱你现在这个样子。哪怕你心里满是污痕和流血的伤口，我也会爱它们、亲吻它们，直到你重新洁净、重新痊愈。我的爱没有别的目的，只想看你变成你一直想成为、也必须成为才能感到幸福的那个人。哪怕你做出再愚蠢的事，我也只会以为你病了，或是有什么古怪的东西毒害了你的心。哪怕你欺骗我，哪怕我见你醉倒在路边，你也照样是我亲爱的珍妮。

「你不愿意做我的人——把自己交给我吗？你不愿意到我怀里来，让我抱着你，让你幸福、让你完整吗？眼下我还不大知道该从何做起，可我的爱会教我；每天早晨你醒来，忧伤都会轻一分——每一天都会显得比前一天亮一点、暖一点，你的悲伤也就小一点。我们到维泰博去，或者任何你喜欢的地方。把你交给我，我要照料你，就像照料一个生病的孩子。等你重新好起来，你就会学着爱上我，并且明白我们两个谁离了谁都活不成。

「你在病中；你照顾不了自己。闭上眼睛，把你的手交给我；我会爱你，把你治好——我知道我做得到。」

===Original===
Jenny was leaning against a pillar. She turned her white face to him, smiling sadly:

“How can you imagine I would do such a great wrong, and sin against God?”

“You mean because you don’t love me? But I tell you it does not matter, because I know my love is such that you will end by returning it, when you have lived wrapped in it for a time.”

He seized her in his arms, covering her face with kisses. She made no resistance, but whispered:

“Don’t, Gunnar, please.”

===Chinese===
珍妮倚着一根廊柱。她把苍白的脸转向他，凄然微笑着：

「你怎么能设想我会做出这样大的错事，在上帝面前犯罪呢？」

「你是说，因为你不爱我？可我告诉你，那不要紧，因为我知道我的爱是这样一种爱：等你在它里面浸润着生活一段时日之后，你终归会回应它的。」

他一把将她搂进怀里，吻遍了她的脸。她没有抗拒，只是低声说：

「别这样，贡纳尔，求你了。」

===Original===
He released her reluctantly:

“Why may I not?”

“Because it is you. I don’t know if I should have minded if it had been anybody else for whom I did not care at all.”

===Chinese===
他不情愿地松开了她：

「为什么不可以？」

「因为是你。若是换一个我毫不在意的旁人，我真不知道自己还会不会介意。」

===Original===
Gunnar held her hand and they walked up and down in the moonlight.

“I understand. When you had the little boy you thought your life had some aim and purpose again after all these aimless years, because you loved him and you needed him. When he died you became indifferent to everything and considered yourself superfluous in the world.”

Jenny nodded:

===Chinese===
贡纳尔握着她的手，两人在月光里踱来踱去。

「我明白。有了那小男孩，你觉得在这么多年漫无目的的日子之后，生活总算又有了目标和意义，因为你爱他，你需要他。他死了，你就对一切都无所谓了，觉得自己在这世上是个多余人。」

珍妮点了点头：

===Original===
“There are a few people I care for enough to be sorry if I knew them in distress and glad if all was well with them. But I myself cannot add either to their sorrow or their joy—it has always been so, and one of the reasons why I was unhappy and filled with longing was just that my life was spent without making anybody happy. My sole wish and yearning was to make another being happy. I have always believed in that as the greatest blessing in life. You spoke of the joy of work—to me it never seemed enough, and it is very selfish, besides, because the greatest joy and satisfaction of it is yours alone; you cannot share it with anybody else. Unless you can share your happiness with others, you lose the greatest possible joy. When you are quite young and feel strongly you are selfish perhaps sometimes—I have felt it myself when I have reached a step nearer my goal, but as a rule it is only the abnormal beings who amass riches for any other purpose than spending. A woman’s life is useless to my mind if she is not the joy of somebody else—and I have never been that—I have only caused sorrow. The little happiness I have been able to give was only what anyone else could have given just as well; they have loved me only for what they imagined me to be, not for my real self.

“After my baby’s death I began to realize how fortunate it was that there was nobody in the world whom I could cause a really inconsolable grief—nobody to whom I was indispensable.

“And now you tell me all this. You have always been the one person I least of all wanted to drag into my confused life; I have always been more fond of you, in a way, than of anyone else I know. I enjoyed our friendship so much, because I thought that love and all it brings in its train could never come between us. You were too good for it, I thought. Oh, how I wish it had never changed!”

===Chinese===
「有几个人，我是关心他们的：知道他们遭了难，我会难过；知道他们一切都好，我会高兴。可我自己呢，既给他们的悲伤添不上什么，也给他们的快乐添不上什么——从来就是这样。我不幸福、心里满是渴念，原因之一正是我的生活没有让任何人幸福过。我唯一的愿望和渴念，就是让另一个人幸福。我一直相信，那是人生最大的福气。你讲到过工作的快乐——在我看它从来不够，况且还很自私，因为其中最大的快乐和满足只属于你一个人；你没法把它分给任何别人。不能与人分享的幸福，就失掉了可能有的最大的快乐。人很年轻、感受又强烈的时候，或许有时会自私——我自己在朝目标走近一步时也有过这种感觉——不过一般说来，只有心智反常的人，才会为了挥霍以外的目的去积攒财富。依我看，一个女人如果不能成为另一个人的快乐，她的一生就是无用的——而我从来没有成为过谁的快乐——我只带来过悲伤。我能给的那一点点幸福，任何别人也都同样给得了；他们爱我，只是爱他们想象中的我，不是真实的我。

「我的孩子死后，我才开始体会到，这世上竟然没有一个会因我而真正伤心欲绝的人——没有一个非我不可的人——这是何等的侥幸。

「如今你却对我说了这些话。你始终是我最最不愿拽进自己这混乱生活里的人；在某种意义上，我一直比喜欢谁都更喜欢你。我们的友谊曾给我那么多快乐，因为我想，爱情和它随之带来的一切，永远不会横到我们中间来。我想，你那样好，是不会沾染那些的。哦，我多希望这一切从未改变！」

===Original===
“To me it seems now that it has never been different,” he said gently. “I love you and you need me. I know I can make you happy again, and when I have done that you will have made me happy.”

Jenny shook her head:

“If I had the least bit of faith in myself left, it would be different. I might have listened to you if I had not felt so keenly that I have done with life. You say you love me, but I know that what you think you love in me is destroyed—dead. It is the same old story: you are in love with some quality you dream that I possess—that I have before or might have acquired. But one day you would see me as I really am, and I should only have made you unhappy too.”

“I should never look upon it as unhappiness whatever my fate might be. You may not be aware of it yourself, but I know that in the state you are now it only needs a touch for you to fall—into something that would be madness. But I love you, and I can see all the way that has led you to it, and if you feel I would follow you to try and carry you back in my arms, because I love you in spite of all.”

===Chinese===
「在我看来，如今这一切和从前并没有什么两样，」他温和地说。「我爱你，而你需要我。我知道我能让你重新幸福起来，而等我做到了，你也就让我幸福了。」

珍妮摇了摇头：

「倘若我心里还剩下一星半点对自己的信心，事情就不同了。要不是我这样痛切地感到，我这一生已经了结，我或许会听你的。你说你爱我，可我知道，你自以为爱着的我身上的那种东西已经毁了——死了。还是那老一套：你爱上的是你梦想中我具备的某种品质——是我从前有过、或者可能养成的。可有朝一日你会看清我真正的样子，到那时，我只是让你也变得不幸罢了。」

「无论我命运如何，我永远不会把它看作不幸。你自己也许没有察觉，可我知道，照你现在这种状态，只消轻轻一推，你就会跌下去——跌进一种疯狂里去。可我爱你，把你引到这一步的那整条路，我都看得清清楚楚；你若觉得我会一路追随你、竭力把你抱回怀中——那是因为，不顾这一切，我依旧爱你。」

===Original===
As they stood by their doors in the dark passage he took her hands: “Jenny, rather than be alone, would you not like me to remain with you tonight?”

She looked at him with a curious smile.

“Oh, Jenny!” He shook his head. “I may come to you, all the same. Would you be angry—or sorry?”

“I think I should be sorry—for your sake. No, do not come, Gunnar. I will not take your love when I know I could just as well give mine to anybody else.”

He laughed a little, half angrily, half sadly.

“Then I ought to do it. If once you were mine you would never belong to anybody else—I know you too well for that—but as you ask me not to, I will wait:” he added, with the same curious little laugh.

===Chinese===
在昏暗的过道里，两人站在各自的房门前，他握起她的双手：「珍妮，与其一个人待着，今晚你不愿意我留下来陪你吗？」

她带着一种古怪的微笑望着他。

「哦，珍妮！」他摇了摇头。「不过我还是可以上你那儿去的。你会生气——还是难过？」

「我想我会难过的——为了你。不，别来，贡纳尔。我知道我的爱反正给谁都一样，这样的情况下，我不愿接受你的爱。」

他笑了一声，一半是气，一半是伤悲。

「那我就该硬去。你一旦是我的了，就永远不会再属于任何别人——这一点我太了解你了——不过既然你叫我别去，那我就等着：」他又添了一句，带着同样古怪的一丝笑。

### XI / 十一

===Original===

Gunnar walked along the narrow road, overgrown with grass, between the high, whitewashed garden walls. On the one side lay the barracks, probably with a terrace, as high up over his head some soldiers were laughing and talking. A tuft of yellow flowers, growing in a cleft of the wall, hung swaying. On the other side of the road the huge old poplars by the Cestius pyramid and the cypress grove in the new part of the cemetery stretched their tops towards the blue and silver clouded sky.

Outside the grated gate a girl sat crocheting. She opened to him, curtseying to thank him for the coin he gave her.

The spring air was mild and damp; in the closed green shade of the churchyard it became wet and warm as in a hothouse, and the narcissus along the border of the path gave out a hot, sickly scent.

The old cypresses stood round the graves that lay, green and dark with creepers and violets, set in terraces from the ivy-clad wall of the town. Above the flowers rose the monuments of the dead, little marble temples, white figures of angels, and big, heavy slabs of stone. Moss grew on them and on the trunks of the cypresses. Here and there a white or red flower still clung to the camelia trees, but most of them lay brown and faded on the black earth, exhaling raw, damp fumes. He remembered something he had read: the Japanese did not like camelias because they fell off whole and fresh like heads chopped off.

Jenny Winge lay buried at the farthest end of the cemetery near the chapel on a grassy slope, covered with daisies. There were only a few graves. On the border of the slope cypresses had been planted, but they were still very small, like toy trees with their pointed green tops on straight brown trunks, reminding one of the pillars in a cloister arcade. Her grave was a little way from the others; it was only a pale grey mound of earth, the grass round it having been trodden down when it was being dug. The sun shone on it and the dark cypresses formed a wall behind it.

===Chinese===

贡纳尔（Gunnar）走在窄窄的路上，路面长满了草，两旁是高高的、刷得雪白的花园围墙。一侧是兵营，想必还带着一座平台，因为他头顶的高处有士兵在谈笑。一簇黄花长在墙缝里，悬在那儿摇曳。路的另一边，塞斯提乌斯金字塔（Cestius pyramid）旁高大的老杨树和墓园新区里的柏树林，把树梢伸向那蓝银云影交织的天空。

铁栅门外坐着一个姑娘，正做着钩针活计。她替他开了门，又屈膝行礼，谢他赏的硬币。

春天的空气温润而潮湿；在这片墓园封闭的绿荫里，空气变得又湿又暖，如同温室里一般，小路两旁的水仙花散出一股燥热甜腻的香气。

老柏树环绕着一座座坟茔；坟上爬满藤蔓和紫罗兰，绿得幽深，从爬满常春藤的城墙起层层叠作台地。花丛之上耸起死者的墓碑：小小的大理石殿堂、白色的天使像，还有又大又厚的石板。石碑上和柏树干上都生了青苔。山茶树上东一朵西一朵地还挂着白花或红花，但大多数已经变成褐色，凋落在黑土上，蒸腾出生冷的湿气。他想起读到过的一句话：日本人不喜欢山茶花，因为山茶花整朵整朵地坠落，还新鲜完好，像被砍下来的头颅。

珍妮·温格（Jenny Winge）葬在墓园尽头小教堂旁的一片草坡上，坡上开满了雏菊。那里只有寥寥几座坟。坡沿上种了柏树，但都还很小，像玩具树，笔直的棕色树干顶着尖尖的绿冠，让人想起修道院回廊里的廊柱。她的坟离别的坟稍远些；不过是一抔灰白的土，坟周的草在动土时被踩倒了。太阳照在坟上，深色的柏树在坟后围成一堵墙。

===Original===

Covering his face with his hands, Gunnar bent on his knees until his head rested on the faded wreaths.

The weariness of spring weighted his limbs, the blood flowed aching with sorrow and regret at every beat of his heavy heart.

Jenny﻿—Jenny﻿—Jenny﻿—he heard her pretty name in every trill of the birds﻿—and she was dead.

Lying far down in the dark. He had cut off a curl from her fair hair and carried it in his pocketbook. He took it out and held it in the sunshine﻿—those poor little filmy threads were the only part of her luxurious, glossy hair the sun could reach and warm.

She was dead and gone. There were some pictures of hers﻿ ﻿… there had been a short notice about her in the papers. And the mother and sisters mourned her at home, but the real Jenny they had never known, and they knew nothing about her life or her death. The others﻿—those who had known﻿—stared in despair after her, not understanding what they knew.

She who lay there was his Jenny; she belonged to him alone.

===Chinese===

贡纳尔双膝跪下，双手掩住脸，俯下身去，直到头抵在褪了色的花圈上。

春的倦怠沉沉坠着他的四肢；他那颗沉重的心每一次搏动，血里都带着哀痛与悔恨，酸痛地流遍全身。

珍妮——珍妮——珍妮——每一声鸟啼里，他都听见她那可爱的名字——而她已经死了。

她躺在幽暗的深处。他曾从她金色的长发上剪下一绺卷发，收在皮夹里。他把它取出来，摊在阳光里——那几缕可怜的、薄纱似的细丝，是她那一头丰茂光润的秀发之中，唯一还能被太阳照到、晒暖的部分。

她死了，一去不返了。她留下了一些画……报上登过一小则讣闻。母亲和妹妹们在家乡为她哀悼，可真正的珍妮她们从不曾认识，对于她的生、她的死，她们一无所知。另一些人——那些知情的人——绝望地目送她而去，却参不透自己知道的一切。

躺在那儿的人，是他的珍妮；她只属于他一个人。

---

===Original===

Helge Gram had come to him; he had asked and told, wailed and begged:

“I don’t understand anything. If you do, Heggen, I beg you to tell me. You know﻿—will you not tell me what you know?”

He had not answered.

“There was another; she told me so. Who was it? Was it you?”

“No.”

“Do you know who it was?”

===Chinese===

海尔格·格拉姆（Helge Gram）来找过他；他问过、说过，哀告过、恳求过：

「我什么都不明白。要是你明白，赫根，我求你告诉我。你知道——你肯把你所知道的告诉我吗？」

他当时没有回答。

「还有另一个人；她是这么对我说的。那人是谁？是你吗？」

「不是。」

「你知道那人是谁吗？」

===Original===

“Yes, but I am not going to tell you. It is no good your asking, Gram.”

“But I shall go mad if you don’t explain.”

“You have no right to know Jenny’s secrets.”

“But why did she do it? Was it because of me﻿—of him﻿—or of you?”

“No; she did it because of herself.”

===Chinese===

「知道，可我不会告诉你。你问也是白问，格拉姆。」

「可你不说个明白，我会发疯的。」

「你没有权利知道珍妮的秘密。」

「可她为什么要那样做？是因为我——因为他——还是因为你？」

「不；她那样做，是因为她自己。」

===Original===

He had asked Gram to leave him and had not seen him since; he had left Rome. It was in the Borghese garden, two days after the funeral, that Gram had come across him sitting in the sunshine. He was so tired; he had had to see to everything﻿—give satisfactory explanations at the inquest, arrange for the interment, and write to Mrs. Berner that her daughter had died suddenly from heart failure. And all the time he had a kind of satisfaction in the thought that nobody knew of his own sorrow, that the real cause of her death was known only by him and forever hidden with him. The sorrow had sunk so deep into him that it would forever be the inmost essence of his soul, and he would never speak of it to any living being.

It would govern his whole life﻿—and be governed by it; the colour and form of it would change, but it would never be effaced. Every hour of the day it was different, but it was always there, and always would be. On the morning when he had run for the doctor, leaving the other man alone with her, he had wanted to tell Helge Gram all he knew, and in such a way that his heart would turn to ashes like his own had done; but in the days that followed all he knew became a secret between him and the dead woman﻿—the secret of their love. All that had happened had happened because of her being what she was, and as such he had loved her. Helge Gram was a casual, indifferent stranger to him and to her, and he had no more wish to avenge himself on him than he had pity for his sorrow and dread at the mystery.

And he understood that what had happened was natural because she was made as she was. Her mind swayed and bent for a gust of wind, because it had grown so upright and slender; he had thought she could grow as a tree grows, and had not understood that she was only a flower, a rich, fragile stem, springing up to be kissed by the sun and to let all the heavy, longing buds break into bloom. She had only been a little girl after all, and to his eternal sorrow he had not understood it until too late.

For she could not right herself again when once she had been bent; she was like a lily, that does not grow from the root again if the first stalk has been broken. There was nothing supple or luxuriant about her mind﻿—but he loved her such as she was. And she was his only, for he alone knew how fair and delicate she had been﻿—so strong in her desire to grow straight, and yet so frail and brittle, and with delicate honour, from which a spot never could be washed away because it made so deep a mark. She was dead. He had been alone with his love many nights and days, and he would be alone with it all the days and nights of his life.

===Chinese===

他当时请格拉姆离开，此后再没有见过他；他离开罗马了。那是葬礼后两天，在博尔盖塞花园（Borghese garden）里，格拉姆撞见他坐在阳光下。他累极了；样样事情都得他去料理——在验尸讯问时给出令人满意的解释，安排下葬，还要写信给伯纳太太（Mrs. Berner），说她的女儿因心力衰竭猝然去世。而这整个过程中，他心底始终怀着一种隐隐的满足：没有人知道他自己的悲伤，她真正的死因只有他一人知道，并将永远随他一同埋藏。这悲伤已沉入他太深的地方，将永远是他灵魂最内里的本质，他永远不会向任何活着的人吐露。

它将支配他的一生——也将受这人生的支配；它的色彩与形貌会改变，却永远不会磨灭。一天里的每个时辰它都不相同，可它始终都在，也将永远都在。那天清晨，他跑去请医生，把另一个男人单独留在她身边；那时他曾想把一切都说给海尔格·格拉姆听，而且要说得让他心也像自己的心一样烧成灰烬；可在随后的日子里，他所知道的一切，变成了他和那死者之间的秘密——他们爱情的秘密。已发生的一切之所以发生，都是因为她生来就是这样的人，而他爱的正是这样的她。海尔格·格拉姆于他、于她，都不过是个偶然的、无关紧要的陌生人；他既无意向他报仇，也不同情他的悲伤和他面对这谜团时的恐惧。

他也明白了，已发生的事是自然的，因为她天生就是这样造就的。一阵风就能让她的心灵摇摆弯折，正因为它长得那样挺拔、那样纤细；他原以为她能像一棵树那样成长，却不明白她只是一朵花，一茎丰盈而脆弱的花枝，拔节而起，只为承受太阳的亲吻，让所有沉甸甸、满怀渴望的花蕾尽数绽放。她终究不过是个小姑娘，而他直到太迟才懂得这一点——这是他永生的哀痛。

因为她一旦被折弯，就再也直不起来了；她像一株百合，第一根花茎若是断了，便不会再从根上抽出新枝。她的心灵里没有半点柔韧丰饶——可他爱的正是她本来的样子。而她是独属于他的，因为唯有他一人知道，她曾经多么美好、多么纤柔——她那样执着地渴望笔直地生长，却又那样孱弱、那样易折，还有那样一份娇嫩的名节，一旦沾上污点便永远洗刷不掉，因为那污痕浸得太深。她死了。许多个日日夜夜，他独自守着自己的爱；而他此生所有的日日夜夜，也都将独自守着它。

===Original===

He had stifled his cries of despair many a night in his pillows. She was dead, and she had never been his. He was the one she should have loved and belonged to, for she was the only one he had ever loved. He had never touched or seen her beautiful, slim, white body that enclosed her soul like a velvet sheath about a thin, feeble blade. Others had possessed it, and had not understood what a strange and rare treasure had happened to fall into their hands. It lay buried in the grave, a prey to ugly change until it was consumed and reduced to a handful of dust in the earth.

Gunnar was shaking with sobs.

Others had loved her, soiled, and destroyed her, not knowing what they did﻿—and she had never been his.

As long as he lived there would be moments when he would feel the same agony about it as now.

Yet he was the only one who owned her at the last; in his hand only would her golden hair sparkle, and she herself was living in him now; her soul and her image were reflected in him clear and firm as in still water. She was dead; she had no more sorrow﻿—it stayed with him instead, to go on living, not to die until he died himself, and because it was living it would grow and change. What it would be like in ten years, he did not know, but it might grow to something great and beautiful.

As long as he lived there would be moments when he would feel the same strange, deep joy that it was so, as he felt now.

===Chinese===

多少个夜晚，他把绝望的呼号闷死在枕头里。她死了，却从来不曾属于过他。他才是她本该爱、本该以身相许的人，因为她是他唯一爱过的人。他从没有触碰过、也从没有见过她那美丽、苗条、白皙的身体——那身体裹着她的灵魂，宛如一柄细弱刀锋外的天鹅绒刀鞘。别人占有了它，却不懂落进自己手中的，是怎样一件奇异而罕见的珍宝。如今它躺在坟墓里，任凭丑恶的腐变作践，直到销蚀净尽，化为泥土中的一撮尘土。

贡纳尔抽泣得浑身发颤。

别人爱过她、玷污过她、毁了她，却不知道自己做了什么——而她从来不曾属于过他。

只要他还活着，就会有一时一刻，为这件事感到与此刻同样的锥心之痛。

然而最后拥有她的，唯有他一人；唯有在他的手中，她金色的头发还会闪光；而她本身，如今就活在他的生命里；她的灵魂与容颜映在他身上，像映在止水中，清晰而稳固。她死了；她不再有悲伤——悲伤留了下来，换到他身上，继续活着，直到他自己死去才会死去；而正因为它活着，它就会生长、会变化。十年之后它会是什么模样，他不知道，但它也许会长成某种伟大而美好的东西。

只要他还活着，就会有一时一刻，为事情竟是这样而感到与此刻同样的、奇异而深沉的喜悦。

===Original===

He remembered dimly what he had been thinking in the early morning hour when he was walking on the terrace overhead while she was ending her life. He had been enraged with her. How could she do it? He had begged and implored to be allowed to help her, to carry her away from the abyss she was nearing, but she had pushed him away and thrown herself down before his eyes﻿—exactly in the way a woman would﻿—an obstinate, irresponsible, foolish way.

When he saw her lying lifeless he had been in despair and rage again, because he would not have let her go. Whatever she had done he would have exonerated her, helped her, offered her his love and trust.

As long as he lived there would be moments when he would reproach her for choosing to die﻿—Jenny, you should not have done it. Yet there would be moments when he would understand that she did so, because it was in keeping with her character, and he would love her for it as long as he lived. And never would he wish that he had not loved her.

But he would cry desperately, as he had already done, because he had not loved her long before; he would cry for the lost years when she had lived beside him as his friend and comrade and he had not understood that she was the woman who should have been his wife. And never would the day dawn when he would wish he had not understood, even if only to see that it was too late.

===Chinese===

他依稀记得那天清晨自己在想些什么：那时他在上方的平台上踱步，而她正在结束自己的生命。他当时对她满腔愤怒。她怎么能这样做？他曾苦苦恳求，求她允许他帮她，把她从正逼近的深渊边带走，可她推开他，当着他的面纵身跳了下去——完完全全是女人家的做法——一种任性、不知轻重、愚蠢的做法。

看到她毫无生气地躺在那里，他再度陷入绝望与狂怒，因为他是决不肯放她去的。无论她做过什么，他都会宽宥她、帮助她、把自己的爱与信任献给她。

只要他还活着，就会有一时一刻，怪她竟选择了死——珍妮，你不该这样做的。然而也会有一时一刻，他会明白她之所以这样做，正是因为合乎她的天性，而且只要他活着，他就会因此爱她。他也永远不愿意自己不曾爱过她。

可他还是会绝望地痛哭，像已经哭过的那样，哭自己没有早得多地爱上她；他要为那些逝去的岁月痛哭——那些年里，她作为朋友和同志生活在他身边，而他竟不明白，她正是本该做他妻子的那个女人。也永远不会有那么一天，他宁愿自己不曾懂得这一点——哪怕懂得了，只剩眼睁睁看着为时已晚。

===Original===

Gunnar rose from his knees. He took a small box from his pocket and opened it. One of Jenny’s pink crystal beads was in it. He had found it in the drawer of her dressing-table when he packed up her belongings; the string had broken and he kept one of the beads. He took some earth from the grave and put it in the box. The bead rolled about in it and was covered with grey dust, but the clear rose colour showed through and the fine rents in the crystal glittered in the sun.

He had sent all her possessions to her mother, except the letters, which he had burnt. The child’s clothes were in a sealed cardboard box. He sent it to Francesca, remembering that Jenny had said one day she would give them to her.

He had looked through all her sketchbooks and drawings before packing them and carefully cut out the leaves with the picture of the boy, hiding them in his pocketbook. They were his﻿—all that was hers alone was his.

On the plain grew some purple anemones; he rose mechanically to pick them. Oh, springtime!

He remembered a spring day two years ago when he had been in Norway. He had been given a cart and a red mare at the posting station; the owner was an old schoolfellow of his. It was a sunny day in March; the meadows were yellow with withered grass, and the dung-heaps over the ploughed fields shone like pale brown velvet. They drove past the familiar farms with yellow, grey, and red houses and apple yards and lilac bushes. The forest all round was olive green, with a purple tint on the birch twigs, and the air was full that day with the chirping of invisible birds.

===Chinese===

贡纳尔从膝上站起身。他从口袋里掏出一只小盒子，打开来。里面是珍妮的一颗粉色水晶珠子。收拾她的遗物时，他在她梳妆台的抽屉里发现了它；串珠的线断了，他留下了其中一颗。他从坟上捧起一点土，放进盒子里。珠子在土里滚来滚去，蒙上了灰色的尘屑，但那澄澈的玫瑰色依然透出来，水晶上细细的裂纹在阳光里熠熠闪亮。

他把她所有的遗物都寄给了她母亲，只有信件除外——那些信他烧掉了。孩子的衣裳装在一只封好的纸盒里。他把它寄给了弗朗切斯卡（Francesca），记得珍妮说过，有朝一日要把这些衣裳送给她。

打包之前，他把她的写生簿和画稿全部翻检了一遍，仔细裁下画着那男孩的那几页，藏进自己的皮夹。那些是他的——凡是独属于她的一切，都是他的。

坡地上开着一些紫色的银莲花；他身不由己地起身去采。哦，春天！

他想起两年前在挪威（Norway）时的一个春日。在驿站里，人家给了他一辆马车和一匹红母马；驿站主人是他的一位老同学。那是三月里一个晴朗的日子；草场上枯草一片金黄，犁过的田野上，一堆堆粪肥泛着浅褐色天鹅绒般的光泽。车子驶过一座座熟悉的农庄，黄的、灰的、红的房子，还有苹果园和丁香丛。四围的森林是橄榄绿的，桦树枝梢带着一抹紫色，那天空气里满是看不见的小鸟的啁啾。

===Original===

Two little fair-haired children were walking in the road, carrying a can. “Where are you going, little ones?”

They stopped, looking suspiciously at him.

“Taking food to father?”

They assented hesitatingly﻿—a little astonished that a strange gentleman should know it.

“Climb up here and I’ll give you a ride.” He helped them into the cart. “Where is father working?”

“At Brusted.”

===Chinese===

两个浅色头发的小孩提着一只罐子在路上走。「你们上哪儿去呀，小家伙？」

他们站住，狐疑地打量他。

「给爸爸送饭去？」

他们迟疑着点了头——有点惊讶，一位陌生的先生居然知道这事。

「爬上来，我捎你们一段。」他把他们抱上马车。「爸爸在哪儿干活呢？」

「在布鲁斯泰德（Brusted）。」

===Original===

“That’s over beyond the school, is it not?”

Thus went the conversation. A stupid, ignorant man asking and asking, as grown-up people always talk to children, and the little ones, who have such a lot of wisdom, consult each other quietly with their eyes, giving sparingly of it﻿—as much as they think convenient.

Hand in hand they walked along beside a rushing brook when he put them down, and he turned his horse in the direction he wanted to go.

There was a prayer meeting that evening at his home. His sister Ingeborg was sitting by the old corner cupboard, following with a pale, ecstatic face and shining steel-blue eyes a shoemaker, who spoke of spiritual grace; then suddenly she rose herself to bear witness.

His pretty, smart sister who once had been so fond of dancing and merriment. She loved to read and to learn; when he had got work in town he sent her books and pamphlets and the Social Democrat twice a week. At thirty she was “saved,” and now she “spoke in tongues.”

===Chinese===

「是在学校那边再过去，对不对？」

谈话就是这样进行的。一个愚蠢无知的大人问个没完，大人同孩子说话向来如此；而那两个一肚子智慧的小家伙，只悄悄用眼睛互相商量，把智慧一点一点吝惜地施舍出来——施多少，全看他们觉得合适。

他把他们放下车时，两个孩子手拉着手，沿着一道奔流的溪边走去了；他掉转马头，朝自己要去的方向走去。

那天晚上，他家里有一个祷告会。他的妹妹英格堡（Ingeborg）坐在老式墙角柜旁，苍白而狂喜的脸上，一双钢蓝色的眼睛灼灼放光，追随着一个讲论属灵恩典的鞋匠；随后她突然自己站起来作见证。

他那漂亮伶俐的妹妹，从前是那样爱跳舞、爱热闹。她喜欢读书，喜欢求知；他进城找到工作后，常给她寄书和小册子，每周两次给她寄《社会民主党人报》（Social Democrat）。三十岁上她「得救」了，如今她会「说方言」了。

===Original===

She had bestowed all her love on her nephew Anders and a little girl they had with them﻿—an illegitimate child from Christiania. With sparkling eyes she told them about Jesus, the children’s Friend.

The next day it was snowing; he had promised to take the children to a cinema in the small neighbouring town, and they had to walk a couple of miles in the melting snow, leaving black footprints behind. He tried to talk to the children, asking questions and receiving guarded answers.

But on the way back the children were the inquisitive ones, and, flattered, he gave frank and detailed answers, anxious to give them proper information about the films they had seen﻿—of cowboys in Arizona and coconut harvest in the Philippines. And he tried his best to tell them properly and answer without ever being at a loss.

===Chinese===

她把全部的爱都倾在外甥安德斯（Anders）和他们带着的一个小女孩身上——那是个私生的孩子，从克里斯蒂安尼亚（Christiania）来的。她眼睛闪闪发光，给孩子们讲耶稣，讲这位孩子们的朋友。

第二天下了雪；他答应过带孩子们到邻近的小镇去看电影，他们只得踩着化雪走上几英里，身后留下一串黑色的脚印。他试着同孩子们攀谈，问一句，得到一句谨慎的答话。

可是在归途上，变成孩子们刨根问底了；他受宠若惊，便答得又坦率又详尽，一心要把他们看过的电影好好讲个明白——亚利桑那（Arizona）的牛仔啦，菲律宾（Philippines）的椰子丰收啦。他尽力好好地讲给他们听，回答得从不迟疑。

---

===Original===

Oh, springtime!

It was on a spring day he had gone to Viterbo with Jenny and Francesca. Dressed in black, she had been sitting by the window looking out with eyes so big and grey﻿—he remembered it well.

The stormy clouds sent showers over the brown plains of the Campagna, where there were no ruins for the tourists to go and see, only a crumbled wall here and there and a farm with two pines and some pointed straw-stacks near the house. The pigs herded together in the valley, where some trees grew beside a stream. The train sped between hills and oak woods where white and blue anemones and yellow primroses grew among the faded leaves. She said she wanted to get out to pick them, gather them in the falling rain among the dripping leaves under the wet branches. “This is like spring at home,” she said.

It had been snowing; some of the snow was still lying grey in the ditches, and the flowers were wet and heavy, with petals stuck together.

===Chinese===

哦，春天！

也是在一个春日，他同珍妮和弗朗切斯卡去了维泰博（Viterbo）。她穿一身黑衣，坐在车窗边望着窗外，那双眼睛那么大、那么灰——他记得清清楚楚。

风雨如晦的云团，把阵阵骤雨泼在坎帕尼亚（Campagna）褐色的平原上；平原上没有废墟供游人来观光，只有东一处西一处的断墙残垣，一座农庄，屋旁两棵松树、几个尖尖的草垛。猪群聚在山谷里，谷中一道溪流边长着几棵树。火车在丘陵和栎树林间飞驰，白色和蓝色的银莲花、黄色的报春花，在枯叶间开着。她说她想下车去采，在落雨中、在滴水的叶子间、在湿漉漉的枝条下把它们采拢来。「这就像家乡的春天。」她说。

那里下过雪；沟渠里还积着灰色的残雪，花朵湿漉漉、沉甸甸，花瓣都粘连在一起。

===Original===

Little rivulets rushed down the crevices, vanishing under the railway line. A shower beat against the window and drove the smoke to earth.

Then it cleared over valleys and grove, and the water streamed down the sides of the hills.

He had had some of his things in one of the girls’ boxes. When he remembered it in the evening they had already begun to undress and were laughing and chatting when he knocked at their door. Jenny opened it a little, giving him what he asked for. She had on a light dressing-jacket with short sleeves, leaving the slender white arm bare. It tempted him to many kisses, but he dared only give it one single, light one. He had been in love with her then, intoxicated with the spring, the wine, the merry rain, and the sudden sunshine, with his own youth and the joy of life. He wanted to make her dance﻿—that tall, fair girl, who smiled so guardedly as if she were trying a new art, something she had never yet done﻿—she who had stared with grey, serious eyes at all the flowers they passed and that she wanted to pick.

Oh, how different all might have been! The dry, bitter sob shook his frame again.

===Chinese===

细细的激流顺着岩缝奔泻而下，钻到铁路底下不见了。一阵骤雨打在车窗上，把烟压向地面。

随后山谷和树丛上空放晴了，雨水顺着山坡滚滚流泻。

他有几件东西寄放在姑娘们的一只箱子里。晚上想起来的时候，她们已经动手卸装就寝；他敲她们的房门时，里面正又说又笑。珍妮把门开了一条缝，把他要的东西递给他。她穿着一件短袖的轻薄晨衣，露着纤细白皙的手臂。那手臂引得他想吻上无数下，可他只敢轻轻地、轻轻地吻了一下。那时他已经爱上她了，为春天、为美酒、为欢畅的雨和骤然放晴的阳光、为自己的青春和生之欢乐而陶醉。他想让她跳舞——那个高挑的金发姑娘，她笑得那样矜持，仿佛在尝试一门新的技艺，一件她从未做过的事——她曾用灰色而严肃的眼睛，凝望一路经过的、她想要采下的所有那些花。

哦，一切本可以多么不同！干涩而苦痛的抽泣，又一次震得他浑身发抖。

===Original===

The day they went to Montefiascone it rained too, so hard that the water was flung back from the stone bridge on to the girls’ lifted skirts and ankles. How they had laughed, all three of them, when they walked up the narrow street, the rainwater streaming towards them in small torrents. When they reached Rocca, the castle cliff in the centre of the old town, it cleared. They leaned over the rampart, looking at the lake, which lay black beneath the olive groves and the vineyards. The skies hung low about the hills round the lake, but across the dark water there came a silver line, which broadened, the mist rolled back into the crevices, and the mountain came into better view. The sun pierced the clouds, which sank down to rest round the small promontories with grey stone castles. Towards the north a distant peak became visible﻿—Cesca said it was Monte Amiata.

The last rain-clouds rolled across the blue spring sky, melting before the sun; the bad weather fled westward, darkening the Etrurian heights, which sloped desolately towards the far yellow-white strip of the Mediterranean. The sight reminded him of the mountain scenery in his own country, in spite of the olive grove and the vineyards.

He and she had stooped behind the hedge, and he had held out his coat so that she could light her cigarette. The wind was keen up there, and she shivered a little in her wet clothes. Her cheeks were red and the sun glittered in her damp, golden hair as she stroked it away from her eyes with one hand.

He would go there tomorrow to meet the spring, the cold, naked spring, full of expectation, with all the buds wet and shivering in the wind﻿—blossoming all the same.

===Chinese===

他们去蒙特菲亚斯科内（Montefiascone）那天也下着雨，雨大得石桥上溅起的水反泼到姑娘们撩起的裙裾和脚踝上。沿着窄窄的街上坡时，雨水汇成一股股小激流朝他们迎面冲来，他们三个人笑得多么厉害啊。走到罗卡（Rocca）——老城中央那座城堡山岩——时，天放晴了。他们倚着胸墙眺望湖水；湖在橄榄园和葡萄园下面，黑沉沉的。天空低低压着湖四周的群山，但一道银线横过暗色的水面，渐渐扩展开来，雾滚退进岩缝里，山的面目渐渐清晰。太阳穿透云层，云沉下来，歇在那些矗立着灰色石堡的小海角四周。朝北面，一座远峰显露出来——切斯卡（Cesca）说，那是阿米亚塔山（Monte Amiata）。

最后的雨云滚过蓝色的春空，在太阳面前消融；坏天气朝西逃去，把伊特鲁里亚（Etrurian）高地罩得阴沉，那片高地荒凉地朝远处地中海（Mediterranean）那黄白色的狭长条带倾伏下去。这景致让他想起自己故乡的山色——尽管这里有橄榄园和葡萄园。

他和她曾在树篱后面躬着身子，他张开外套替她挡风，好让她点着香烟。上面风很尖利，她穿着湿衣裳，微微发抖。她的脸颊红红的，太阳在她潮湿的金发上闪闪发亮，她一只手把头发从眼睛上掠开。

明天他要到那边去迎春天，那寒冷的、赤裸的春天，满怀期待，所有的花蕾都湿着、在风里颤栗——却照样要开花。

---

===Original===

She and the spring were one to him, she who stood shivering and smiled in the changing weather and wanted to gather all the flowers into her lap. Little Jenny, you were not to gather the flowers, and your dreams never came true﻿—and now I am dreaming them for you.

And when I have lived long enough to be so full of longing as you were, perhaps I will do as you, and say to fate: Give me a few of the flowers; I will be satisfied with much less than I wanted in the beginning of life. But I will not die as you did, because you could not be content. I will remember you, and kiss your head and your golden hair and think: She could not live without being the best, and claiming the best as her right; and maybe I shall say: Heaven be praised that she chose death rather than living content.

Tonight I will go to Piazza San Pietro and listen to the wild music of the fountain that never stops, and dream my dream. For you, Jenny, are my dream, and I have never had any other.

===Chinese===

在他心里，她和春天是一体的；她站在变幻不定的天气里微微发抖，微笑着，想把所有的花都揽进自己怀里。小珍妮，你没能采到那些花，你的梦一个也没有成真——如今，我替你把它们梦下去。

等我活得够久，久到心里也充满你那样的渴望时，也许我会学你的样子，对命运说：给我几朵花吧；比起我人生之初想要的，少得多我也就满足了。但我不会像你那样去死，因为你不能甘心。我会记着你，吻你的头、你金色的头发，心里想着：她生来不肯屈居其次，认定最好的一切是自己应得的权利；也许我还会说：感谢上天，她宁可选择了死，也不肯心满意足地活下去。

今晚我要到圣彼得广场（Piazza San Pietro）去，听那永不停歇的喷泉奏出的狂野音乐，去做我的梦。因为你，珍妮，就是我的梦，我从来不曾有过别的梦。

---

===Original===

Dream﻿—oh, dream!

If your child had lived he would not have been what you dreamt when you held him in your arms. He might have done something good and great, or something bad and disgraceful, but he would never have accomplished what you dreamt he should do. No woman has given life to the child she dreamt of when she bore it﻿—no artist has created the work he saw before him in the moment of his inspiration. And we live summer after summer, but not one is like the one we have been longing for when we stooped to gather the wet flowers in the spring showers. And no love is what lovers dreamed when they kissed for the first time.

If you and I had lived together we might have been happy or not, we might have done good or ill to one another, but I shall never know what our love would have been if you had been mine. The only thing I know is that it would never have been what I dreamt that night when I stood with you in the moonlight while the fountain was playing.

And yet I would not have missed that dream, and I would not miss the dream I am dreaming now.

Jenny, I would give my life if you could meet me on the cliff and be as you were then, and kiss me and love me for one day, one hour. Always I am thinking of what it might have been if you had lived and been mine, and it seems to me that a boundless joy has been wasted. Oh, you are dead, and your death has made me so poor. I have only my dream of you, but if I compare my poverty with others’ riches it is ever so much more glorious. Not to save my life would I cease to love you and dream of you and mourn you.

===Chinese===

梦啊——哦，梦！

倘使你的孩子活了下来，他也不会成为你抱着他时梦想的那个样子。他也许做成过什么善良而伟大的事，也许做过什么邪恶而不光彩的事，但他永远做不成你梦想他要做成的事。没有哪个女人，生下过她孕育时梦想的孩子——也没有哪个艺术家，创作出过灵感袭来的那一刻他眼前看见的作品。我们一个夏天接一个夏天地活着，却没有一个夏天，像我们在春雨中俯身采撷湿花时所渴望的那个夏天。也没有哪一场爱情，是恋人们初次接吻时所梦想的爱情。

倘使你和我一起生活过，我们也许幸福，也许不幸福，也许彼此相益，也许彼此相伤；可你若曾属于我，我们的爱究竟会是什么样子，我永远无从知晓了。我只知道一件事：它永远不会是我梦想的那个样子——那夜我同你站在月光下，喷泉正喷涌着。

然而我不悔做过那个梦，也不肯错过此刻正做的这个梦。

珍妮，只要你能到山崖上来同我相会，像当年那样，吻我、爱我，哪怕一天，哪怕一个钟头，我情愿拿我的生命去换。我总在想，倘若你活着、属于我，一切本会是什么样子；在我看来，一场无边的欢喜就这样被虚掷了。哦，你死了，你的死叫我变得这样贫穷。我只剩下我对你的梦；可若拿我的贫穷同别人的富有相比，它却辉煌得多。哪怕舍了我的性命，我也不会停止爱你、梦你、为你哀悼。

---

===Original===

Gunnar Heggen did not know that in the great storm of his heart he had lifted his arms towards heaven and was whispering to himself. The anemones he had picked were still in his hand, but he did not know it.

The soldiers on the wall laughed at him, but he did not see. He pressed the flowers to his heart, and whispered gently to himself as he walked slowly from the grave toward the cypress grove.

===Chinese===

贡纳尔·赫根（Gunnar Heggen）并不知道，在内心那场大风暴中，他已朝着天空举起了双臂，正喃喃自语。他采下的银莲花还握在手里，可他自己并不知道。

墙上的士兵们笑话他，可他没有看见。他把花按在胸口，缓缓地从坟前朝柏树林走去，一路轻轻地自语着。

### I / 一

===Original===
As Helge Gram turned the corner into Via Condotti in the dusk a military band came down the street playing “The Merry Widow” in such a crazy, whirling time that it sounded like wild bugle calls. The small, dark soldiers rushed past in the cold afternoon, more like a Roman cohort intent on attacking barbarian hosts than peaceful men returning to their barracks for supper. That was perhaps the cause of their haste, Helge thought, smiling to himself, for as he stood there watching them, his coat-collar turned up for the cold, a peculiar atmosphere of history had pervaded him—but suddenly he found himself humming the same tune, and continued his way in the direction where he knew the Corso lay.

He stopped at the corner and looked. So that was the Corso—an endless stream of carriages in a crowded street, and a surging throng of people on a narrow pavement.

He stood still, watching the stream run past him, and smiled at the thought that he could drift along this street every evening in the dusk among the crowds, until it became as familiar to him as the best-known thoroughfare of his own city—Christiania. He was suddenly seized with the wish to walk and walk—now and all night maybe—through all the streets of Rome, for he thought of the town as it had appeared to him a while ago when he was looking down on it from Pincio, while the sun was setting.

Clouds all over the western sky, close together like small pale grey lambkins, and as the sun sank behind him it painted their linings a glorious amber. Beneath the pale skies lay the city, and Helge understood that this was the real Rome—not the Rome of his imagination and his dreams, but Rome as she actually was.

===Chinese===
暮色里，当海尔格·格拉姆（Helge Gram）拐过街角走上孔多蒂街（Via Condotti）时，一支军乐队沿街奏着《风流寡妇》走来，节拍疯狂回旋，听着竟像狂野的号角声。那些矮小黝黑的士兵在寒冷的午后匆匆跑过，与其说是回营房吃晚饭的太平军人，不如说更像一支要去攻打蛮族大军的罗马步兵。他们走得这么急，兴许正是这个缘故吧，海尔格暗自失笑地想；因为当他竖起衣领站在那里望着队伍时，一种奇异的、历史的氛围已经浸透了他——可突然间他发觉自己竟哼起了同一支曲子，便继续朝他知道的科尔索（Corso）大街的方向走去。

他在街角停下脚步，举目望去。这就是科尔索大街了——拥挤的街道上车流无穷无尽，窄窄的人行道上人潮涌动。

他伫立不动，望着这股人流从身边奔涌而过，想到今后每天傍晚他都可以在暮色里、在人群中顺着这条街信步游荡，直到它变得像自己城市里那条最熟悉的大街——克里斯蒂安尼亚（Christiania）的大街——一样亲切，他不由微笑起来。他忽然生出一股冲动，想走啊走啊——也许就从现在起走上一整夜——走遍罗马（Rome）所有的街道；因为他想起了不久前太阳西沉时，自己从平乔（Pincio）上俯瞰这座城的模样。

西天布满了云，一朵朵紧挨在一起，像一只只灰白的小羊羔；太阳在他背后沉落下去，把云的衬里染成一片绚烂的琥珀色。城市就卧在苍白的天空下，海尔格明白了，这才是真正的罗马——不是他想象中、梦里的罗马，而是罗马本来的面目。

===Original===
Everything else he had seen on his journey had disappointed him, for it was not what he had imagined at home when he had been longing to go abroad and see it all. One sight at last was far beyond his dreams, and that was Rome.

A plain of housetops lay beneath him in the valley, the roofs of houses new and old, of houses high and low—it looked as if they had been built anywhere and at any time, and of a size to suit the need of the moment. In a few places only a space could be seen between the mass of housetops, as of streets. All this world of reckless lines, crossing each other in a thousand hard angles, was lying inert and quiet under the pale skies, while the setting sun touched the borders of the clouds with a tinge of light. It was dreaming under a thin veil of white mist, which no busy pillar of smoke dared penetrate, for no factory chimney could be seen, and no smoke came from a single one of the funny little chimney pipes protruding from the houses. The round, old, rust-brown tiles were covered by greyish moss, grass and small plants with yellow blossoms grew in the gutters; along the border of the terraces the aloes stood immovably still in their tubs, and creepers hung in dead cascades from the cornices. Here and there the upper part of a high house rose above its neighbour, its dark, hollow windows staring at one out of a grey or reddish-yellow wall, or sleeping behind closed shutters. Loggias stood out of the mist, looking like parts of an old watchtower, and small summerhouses of wood or corrugated iron were erected on the roofs.

Above it all masses of church cupolas were floating—the huge, grey one, far on the other side of what Helge supposed to be the river, was that of St. Peter.

Beyond the valley, where the roofs covered the silent city—it well deserved the epithet “eternal” tonight—a low hill stretched its longish back toward the skies, carrying on the faraway ridge an avenue of pines, the foliage of which formed one large mass above the row of slender trunks. And behind the dome of St. Peter the eye was arrested by another hill with villas, built among pines and cypresses. Probably Monte Mario.

===Chinese===
旅途上他所见的一切都叫他失望，因为那全不是他当初在国内一心渴望出国看个究竟时所想象的样子。终于有一景远远超出了他的梦想，那就是罗马。

谷地在他脚下铺开一片屋顶的平原，新屋旧屋、高楼低房的屋顶——看上去仿佛是随随便便在哪儿、随随便便什么时候盖起来的，尺寸大小也只求应付一时之需。只有在几处地方，才能从这一大片屋顶之间看出街道的空隙。这整个由放纵横斜的线条构成的世界，彼此交叉出千万个生硬的角度，在苍白的天空下沉沉地、静静地躺着；西沉的太阳给云的边缘抹上一线微光。它罩在一层薄薄的白雾轻纱下做着梦，没有一根匆忙的烟柱胆敢闯进这层纱——因为看不见一根工厂的烟囱，那些从房顶上探出来的有趣的小烟囱管，也一概不冒烟。圆圆的、锈棕色的旧瓦上覆着灰苔，天沟里长着草和开小黄花的植物；沿着平台的边沿，芦荟栽在木桶里纹丝不动地立着，藤蔓从檐口垂挂下来，像一道道凝滞的瀑布。不时有一座高楼的上部从邻舍的头顶探出来，黑洞洞的凹窗从灰色或红黄色的墙上瞪视着人，或是闭着百叶窗酣睡。一座座凉廊从雾中探出身来，看上去像古老瞭望塔的一部分，屋顶上还搭着木头或波形铁皮的小凉亭。

在这一切之上，浮着一大团一大团教堂的穹顶——那顶巨大的灰色穹顶，在海尔格猜想是河的对岸远处，正是圣彼得（St. Peter）教堂的。

在山谷之外，屋顶覆盖着那座沉默的城——今晚它实在当得起「永恒」这个称号——一座低低的山丘把它长长的脊背伸向天空，远远的山脊上托着一排松树的林荫道，繁茂的枝叶在修长树干的那一排之上连成浩浩的一片。而在圣彼得穹顶的后面，还有一座带着别墅的山峦攫住了人的目光，别墅散落在松树和柏树之间。多半是蒙特马里奥（Monte Mario）吧。

===Original===
The dark leaves of the holly formed a roof over his head, and behind him a fountain made a curiously living sound as the water splashed against the stone border, before flowing into the basin beneath it.

Helge whispered to the city of his dreams, whose streets his feet had not yet touched, whose houses did not harbour one single soul he knew: “Rome—Rome—eternal Rome.” He was suddenly struck by his own loneliness and startled at his emotion, though he knew that there was nobody to witness it, and, turning round, he hurried down the Spanish stairs.

And now when he stood at the corner of Condotti and Corso he experienced a quaint and yet pleasant anxiety at the thought of mixing with those hustling crowds and finding his way in the strange city—to wander through it as far as Piazza San Pietro.

As he was crossing the street two young girls passed him. They looked like Norwegians, he thought, with a slight thrill of pleasure. One of them was very fair and wore light-coloured furs.

===Chinese===
冬青深暗的叶子在他头顶搭成一片绿荫，身后一座喷泉发出一种出奇地富有生气的声响——水花溅在石沿上，再流进下面的水池里去。

海尔格对着这座梦中之城喃喃低语——它的街道他的脚步还不曾踏上，它的屋宇下没有收留一个他认识的人：「罗马——罗马——永恒的罗马。」他忽然被自己的孤独击中了，又为自己的动情吃了一惊，虽然明知并没有谁会看见，还是转过身去，匆匆走下了西班牙台阶。

此刻他站在孔多蒂街和科尔索大街的街角，一想到就要汇入那些熙攘的人流，在这座陌生的城市里摸索寻路——要一路走到圣彼得广场（Piazza San Pietro）去——心里便生出一种古怪却又叫人愉快的惶惑。

他正穿过街去，两个年轻姑娘从他身旁走过。她们像是挪威人，他心想，心头掠过一丝淡淡的喜悦。其中一个生得十分白皙，披着浅色的毛皮。

===Original===
It was a joy to him even to read the names of the streets carved in clear, Latin type on white marble slabs set in the corners of the houses.

The street he took ran into an open space near a bridge, on which two rows of lanterns burned with a sickly, greenish flame in the pale light pouring down from the restless sky. A low parapet of stone ran along the waterline, bordered by a row of trees with faded leaves and trunks, dropping their bark in big white flakes. On the opposite side of the river the street lamps were burning among the trees, and the houses stood out black against the sky, but on this side the twilight still flickered on the windowpanes. The sky was almost clear now, and hung transparent and greenish blue over the hill with the pine avenue, with here and there a few reddish, threatening, slowly moving clouds.

He stopped on the bridge and looked down into the Tiber. How dull the water was! It flowed on rapidly, reflecting the colours of the evening skies, sweeping twigs and gravel and bits of wood on its way between the stone walls. A small staircase on the side of the bridge led down to the water’s edge. Helge thought how easy it would be to walk down the steps one night, when one was tired of everything—had any ever done so? he wondered.

He asked a policeman the way to St. Peter’s cathedral in German; the man answered him first in French and then Italian, and when Helge repeatedly shook his head, he spoke French again, pointing up the river. Helge turned in that direction.

===Chinese===
就连读一读街名，于他也是一桩乐事——那名字用清晰的拉丁字母刻在白色大理石板上，嵌在房屋的墙角上。

他走的那条街通到一座桥附近的开阔地上；桥上两行灯笼在从躁动不安的天空泼洒下来的苍白光线里，燃着病恹恹的、发绿的火苗。一道低矮的石沿顺着水岸延伸，沿岸一排树的叶子已经凋败，树干上的皮大片大片地剥落，露出白色。河对岸的街灯在树丛中亮着，房屋衬着天空显出黑色的轮廓；而这一边，暮色还在窗玻璃上闪烁。天空此刻几乎晴朗了，透明地、青蓝地悬在那一带松林荫道的山丘上，只是这里那里还浮着几朵泛红的、带着威胁意味的、缓缓移动的云。

他在桥上停下，俯望台伯河（Tiber）。这河水多么晦暗！它湍急地流着，映出傍晚天空的颜色，卷着树枝、砾石和木片，从石墙之间流过去。桥侧有一道小小的石阶梯通到水边。海尔格想，哪天夜里，当一个人对一切都厌倦了的时候，顺着这石阶走下去，该是多么容易的事——可曾真有人这样做过么？他暗自纳闷。

他用德语向一名警察打听去圣彼得大教堂的路；那人先用法语、后用意大利语回答他，见海尔格只是一再摇头，便又说起法语来，朝河的上游指了指。海尔格便朝那个方向走去。

===Original===
A huge, dark stone erection stood out against the sky, a low, round tower with a jagged crest and the jet-black silhouette of an angel on top. He recognized the lines of the San Angelo fort, and went close up to it. It was still light enough for the statues by the bridge to show up yellow in the twilight, the red skies were still mirrored in the flowing waters of the Tiber, but the street lamps had gained power, and threw out paths of light across the river. Beyond the San Angelo bridge the electric tramcars with illuminated windows rolled over the new iron bridge, throwing white sparks from the connecting wires.

Helge took off his hat to a man:

“*San Pietro, favorisca?*”

The man pointed with his finger and said something Helge did not understand. He turned into a dark and narrow street which, with a sensation of joy, he almost thought he recognized, for it was exactly like the Italian street of his imagination: shop after shop full of curios. He gazed into the poorly lit windows. Most of the things were rubbish—those dirty strips of coarse white lace hanging on a string were surely not Italian handiwork. There were bits of pottery exhibited in dusty box-lids and small bronze figures of a poisonous green, old and new brass candlesticks and brooches with heaps of stones that looked far from genuine. Yet he was seized by a senseless wish to go in and buy something—to inquire, to bargain, and to purchase. Almost before he knew it, he had entered a small, stuffy shop filled with all sorts of things. There were church-lamps hanging from the ceiling, bits of silk with gold flowers on red and green and white ground, and broken pieces of furniture.

===Chinese===
天幕上矗立着一座黑黢黢的庞大石头建筑：一座低矮的圆塔，顶上是一圈锯齿状的齿饰，再顶上是一个漆黑的天使剪影。他认出了圣天使堡（San Angelo）的轮廓，便走近前去。天色还算亮，桥边的雕像在暮色里泛出黄色，红色的天空还映在台伯河流动的水面上，可街灯已经亮足了劲儿，把一道道光带投过河面。圣天使桥的那一边，亮着车窗的电车从新铁桥上驶过，连接的电线上甩出一道道白色火花。

海尔格向一位先生脱了脱帽：

『San Pietro, favorisca?』（圣彼得教堂，劳驾？）

那人用手指了指，说了句海尔格听不懂的话。他拐进一条昏暗的窄街，心里带着一阵欢喜，几乎以为自己认得这条路——因为它同他想象中的意大利街道一模一样：一家接一家摆满古玩的铺子。他凝望着那些灯光暗淡的橱窗。大部分东西都是破烂货——挂在绳子上那些脏兮兮的粗白花边布条，准不是意大利的手工。蒙尘的匣盖里陈列着陶瓷碎片，还有泛着毒绿色的青铜小人像、新旧不一的黄铜烛台，以及缀着一堆堆怎么看也不像真货的石头的胸针。然而他却被一种没有来由的欲望攫住，想进去买点什么——问问价，讲讲价，买下一件。几乎不等他自己明白过来，他已经走进一家闷塞的小铺子，里面堆满了各式各样的东西：天花板上挂着教堂用的灯，有红、绿、白底子上织金花的丝绸零头，还有残破的旧家具。

===Original===
Behind the counter a youth with a dark complexion and a bluish, unshaven chin was reading. He talked and asked questions while Helge pointed at various articles, “*Quanto?*” The only thing he understood was that the prices were excessive, but one ought not to buy until one knew the language well enough to bargain with them.

Several pieces of china were standing on a shelf, rococo figures and vases with sprays of roses, which looked quite modern. Helge seized one at random and placed it on the counter: “*Quanto?*”

“*Sette*,” said the youth, and spread out seven fingers.

“*Quattro*,” said Helge, holding out four fingers in a new brown glove, and felt quite pleased with himself at this leap into the foreign language. He did not understand one word of the man’s arguments, but each time he finished talking Helge raised his four fingers and repeated his quattro, adding with a superior air: “*Non antica!*”

===Chinese===
柜台后面坐着一个肤色黝黑的青年，下巴上泛着青色的胡茬，正在读书。海尔格指着各色物件，说一声『Quanto?』（多少钱？），那青年便一边搭话、一边反问个不停。海尔格唯一听懂的是价钱贵得离谱——不过，语言还没学到能跟他们讨价还价的地步，本来就不该急着买东西。

架子上摆着几件瓷器——洛可可（rococo）风格的人像和绘着玫瑰花枝的花瓶，看上去相当新式。海尔格随手抓起一件，放到柜台上：『Quanto?』（多少钱？）

『Sette』（七），青年说着，张开了七根手指。

『Quattro』（四），海尔格说着，从崭新的棕色手套里伸出四根手指，为自己这纵身一跳闯进外语世界的壮举颇为得意。那人的滔滔雄辩他一个字也听不懂，但每次对方一住口，他就举起四根手指，把他的『Quattro』（四）再重复一遍，还带着居高临下的神气补上一句：『Non antica!』（不是古货！）

===Original===
But the shopkeeper protested, “*Si, antica.*” “*Quattro*,” said Helge again—the man had now only five fingers in the air—and turned towards the door. The man called him back, accepting, and Helge, feeling highly pleased with himself, went out with his purchase wrapped up in pink tissue paper.

He perceived the dark mass of the church at the bottom of the street outlined against the sky, and walked on. He hurried across the first part of the piazza with its lighted shop windows and passing trams towards the two semicircular arcades, which laid a pair of rounded arms, as it were, about one part of the place, drawing it into the quiet and darkness of the massive church, with its broad steps extending in a shell-like formation far out on the piazza.

The dome of the church and the row of saints along the roof of the arcades stood out black against the faint light of the sky; the trees and houses on the hill at the back seemed to be heaped one on top of the other in an irregular fashion. The street lamps were powerless here, the darkness streamed forth between the pillars, and spread over the steps from the open portico of the church. He went slowly up the steps close to the church and looked through the iron doors. Then he went back again to the obelisk in the middle of the piazza and stood there gazing at the dark building. He bent his head back, and followed with his eyes the slender needle of stone that pointed straight into the evening sky, where the last clouds had descended on the roofs of that part of the town whence he had come, and the first radiant sparks of the stars pierced the gathering darkness.

Again his ears caught the sound of water emptying into a stone cistern, and the soft ripple of the overflow from one receptacle into another into the basin. He approached one of the fountains and watched the thick, white jet, driven upwards as it were in angry defiance and looking black against the clear atmosphere, to break high in the air and sink back into the darkness, where the water gleamed white again. He kept staring at it until a gust of wind took hold of the jet and bent it towards him, raining icy drops on his face, but he remained where he was, listening and staring. Then he walked a few steps—stood still—and walked again, but very slowly, listening to an inner voice. It was true, then—really true—that he was here, far, far away from everything he had longed so intensely to leave. And he walked still more slowly, furtively, like one who has escaped from prison.

===Chinese===
可店主抗辩道：『Si, antica.』（是古货。）『Quattro』（四），海尔格又说了一遍——这会儿那人悬在空中的手指只剩五根了——说罢转身朝门口走去。店主连忙把他唤回来，认了价。海尔格心花怒放，扬扬得意地走出店门，买下的东西已用粉色棉纸包好。

他望见街尽头教堂黑压压的一大团映在天幕上，便继续前行。他匆匆穿过广场的头一段——那里橱窗亮着灯、电车来往穿行——走向那两道半圆形的柱廊；柱廊仿佛伸出两条浑圆的臂膀，环抱住广场的一翼，把它拢进大教堂深沉的静谧与幽暗里；教堂宽阔的台阶呈扇贝形，一直铺展到广场上很远的地方。

教堂的穹顶和柱廊檐沿那一排圣徒像，衬着微亮的天色显出黑色；后面山丘上的树木和房屋，似乎乱七八糟地一堆摞着一堆。街灯在这里无能为力了，黑暗从柱子之间流泻出来，又顺着教堂敞开的门廊漫过台阶。他沿着紧靠教堂的台阶缓缓走上去，隔着铁门向里张望。随后他又走回广场中央的方尖碑下，站在那儿凝望那座黑黢黢的建筑。他仰起头，目光顺着那根笔直指向傍晚天空的细长石针望去；他来的那片城区的屋顶上，最后几朵云已经降落下来，而最初几点熠熠的星光，正刺破愈聚愈浓的黑暗。

他的耳边又响起水注入石槽的声响，还有溢出的水从一层石盘漫过另一层、再落进池水里去的轻柔淙淙声。他走近一座喷泉，望着那粗壮的白色水柱——它仿佛怀着怒气、带着挑衅一般被逼向上空，在澄澈的夜气里显得黑沉沉的，到高空中訇然折断，又跌回暗处，水在那里重新闪出白光。他一直怔怔地望着，直到一阵风攥住水柱，把它朝他这边压过来，冰冷的雨点洒了他一脸，他却仍站在原地，听着，望着。然后他走了几步——站住——又走起来，走得很慢很慢，倾听着心里的一个声音。这是真的了——果真是真的——他真的到了这里，远远地、远远地离开了他曾那样急切地想要抛开的一切。他走得愈发慢了，怯生生地，像一个越狱逃出来的人。

===Original===
At the corner of the street there was a restaurant. He made for it, and on his way found a tobacco shop, where he bought some cigarettes, picture cards and stamps. Waiting for his steak, he drank big gulps of claret, while he wrote to his parents; to his father: “I have been thinking of you very often today”—it was true enough—and to his mother: “I have already got a small present for you, the first thing I bought here in Rome.” Poor mother—how was she? He had often been impatient with her these last years. He unpacked the thing and had a look at it—it was probably meant for a scent-bottle. He added a few words to his mother’s card that he managed the language all right, and that to bargain in the shops was an easy matter.

The food was good, but dear. Never mind, once he was more at home here he would soon learn how to live cheaply. Satisfied and exhilarated by the wine, he started to walk in a new direction, past long, low, dilapidated houses, through an archway on to a bridge. A man in a barrier hut stopped him and made him understand that he had to pay a soldo. On the other side of the bridge was a large, dark church with a dome.

He got into a labyrinth of dark, narrow bits of streets—in the mysterious gloom he surmised the existence of old palaces with projecting cornices and lattice windows side by side with miserable hovels, and small church-fronts in between the rows of houses. There were no pavements and he stepped into refuse that lay rotting in the gutter. Outside the narrow doors of the lighted taverns and under the few street lamps he had a vague glimpse of human forms.

He was half delighted, half afraid—boyishly excited, and wondering at the same time how he was to get out of this maze and find the way to his hotel at the ends of the earth—take a cab, he supposed.

===Chinese===
街角上有一家餐馆。他朝它走去，半路上遇见一家烟店，买了些香烟、画片和邮票。等牛排的工夫，他大口大口地喝着红葡萄酒，一面给父母写信；给父亲写的是：「今天我常常想起你」——这倒不假——给母亲写的是：「我已经给你买到一件小礼物，是我在罗马买的头一件东西。」可怜的母亲——她如今怎么样了？这几年，他常常对她不耐烦。他把那件东西拆开来看了看——它大概原是打算做香水瓶的吧。他又在给母亲的明信片上添了几句，说自己语言应付得来，在铺子里讨价还价也是小事一桩。

饭菜不错，就是贵。不要紧，等在这儿住熟了，他很快就会学会省钱的过日子法。吃得心满意足，又被酒催得兴冲冲，他循着一个新的方向走去，经过一长排低矮破败的房子，穿过一道拱门，上了一座桥。栅栏小屋里一个人拦住他，让他明白必须付一个索尔多（soldo）。桥的那一边是一座带穹顶的、黑黢黢的大教堂。

他走进了一座由昏暗狭窄的街巷织成的迷宫——在神秘的幽暗里，他隐约辨出一些檐口凸出、装着花格窗的古老宫殿，同破烂的陋屋并肩而立，一排排房屋中间还夹着小小的教堂门面。这里没有人行道，他一脚踩进了沟里腐烂的垃圾。亮着灯的小酒馆窄门外、稀稀几盏路灯下，人影影影绰绰。

他一半欢喜，一半害怕——孩子气地兴奋着，同时又捉摸着怎样才能走出这座迷宫，找到回那个远在天边的旅馆的路——他想，雇辆马车吧。

===Original===
He passed down another narrow, almost empty street. A small strip of clear, blue sky was visible between the high houses with their frameless windows, looking like black holes cut in the wall. On the uneven stone bridge dust and straw and bits of paper were tossed about by a light gust of wind.

Two women, walking behind him, passed him close under a lamp. He gave a start: they were the ones he had noticed that afternoon in the Corso and believed to be Norwegian. He recognized the light furs of the taller one.

Suddenly he felt an impulse to try an adventure—to ask them the way, so as to hear if they were Norwegian—or Scandinavian at any rate, for they were certainly foreigners. With slightly beating heart he started to walk after them.

The two young girls stopped outside a shop, which was closed, and then walked on. Helge wondered if he should say “Please” or “*Bitte*” or “*Scusi*”—or if he should blurt out at once “*Undskyld*”—it would be funny if they were Norwegians.

===Chinese===
他又穿过另一条几乎空无一人的窄街。高高的房屋之间露出窄窄一条清澈的蓝天；那些房子上没有窗框的窗，像在墙上挖出的一个个黑洞。凹凸不平的石桥上，一阵轻风把尘土、稻草和纸屑吹得团团打转。

两个女子从他身后走来，在一盏灯下紧挨着他走了过去。他一怔：这正是他下午在科尔索大街上注意到、猜想是挪威人的那两位。他认出了高个儿那位身上的浅色毛皮。

他忽然生出一股冲动，想试一次冒险——向她们问路，好听听她们究竟是不是挪威人——至少总归是斯堪的纳维亚人，因为她们分明是外国人了。他心跳得微微加快，抬脚跟了上去。

两个年轻姑娘在一家铺子门前停下——铺子已经打烊——接着又往前走。海尔格心里盘算，开口该说一声「Please」（请），还是『Bitte』（德语「请」），或者『Scusi』（意语「劳驾」）——还是干脆一开口就脱口一句『Undskyld』（丹麦语「请原谅」）——她们要是当真是挪威人，那就有趣了。

===Original===
The girls turned a corner; Helge was close upon them, screwing up courage to address them. The smaller one turned round angrily and said something in Italian in a low voice. He felt disappointed and was going to vanish after an apology, when the tall one said in Norwegian: “You should not speak to them, Cesca—it is much better to pretend not to notice.”

“I cannot bear that cursed Italian rabble; they never will leave a woman alone,” said the other.

“I beg your pardon,” said Helge, and the two girls stopped, turning round quickly.

“I hope you will excuse me,” he muttered, colouring, and, angrily conscious of it, blushed still deeper. “I only arrived from Florence today, and have lost my way in these winding streets. I thought you were Norwegian, or at any rate Scandinavian, and I cannot manage the Italian language. Would you be kind enough to tell me where to find a car? My name is Gram,” he added, raising his hat again.

===Chinese===
姑娘们拐过街角；海尔格紧跟着她们，鼓起勇气要开口。个子较小的那位气恼地转过身来，用意大利语低低说了句什么。他大失所望，正想道声歉便悄悄走开，却听高个儿的那位用挪威语说道：「你不该搭理他们，切斯卡（Cesca）——只当没有察觉才好。」

「我受不了那帮可恶的意大利贱民；他们从来不肯放过一个女人。」另一位说。

「请原谅。」海尔格说。两位姑娘停住脚步，飞快地转过身来。

「希望你们别见怪。」他讷讷地说，脸红了，又恼恨地意识到这一点，脸红得更深了。「我今天刚从佛罗伦萨（Florence）来，在这些弯弯绕绕的街巷里迷了路。我见你们像是挪威人，至少是斯堪的纳维亚人，可意大利话我又对付不来。能不能劳驾告诉我，在哪儿能找到电车？我叫格拉姆。」他补上一句，又举了举帽子。

===Original===
“Where do you live?” asked the taller girl.

“At a place called the Albergo Torino, close to the station,” he explained.

“He should take the Trastevere tram at San Carlo ai Catenari,” said the other.

“No; better take a No. 1 at the new Corso.”

“But those cars don’t go to the Termini,” answered the little one.

===Chinese===
「你住在哪儿？」高个儿姑娘问。

「住在一个叫都灵旅馆（Albergo Torino）的地方，挨着车站。」他解释道。

「他该在圣卡洛-阿伊-卡泰纳里（San Carlo ai Catenari）坐特拉斯提弗列（Trastevere）线的电车。」另一位说。

「不对，还是在新的科尔索大街上坐1路车好。」

「可那些车不去特米尼（Termini）车站呀。」小个子姑娘应道。

===Original===
“Yes, they do. Those that have San Pietro, *stazione Termini*, written on them,” she explained to Helge.

“Oh, that one! It runs past Capo le Case and Ludovisi and an awful long way about first—it will take an hour at least to the station with that one.”

“No, dear; it goes direct—straight along Via Nazionale.”

“It does not,” insisted the other; “it goes to the Lateran first.”

The taller girl turned to Helge: “The first turning right will take you into a sort of market. From there you go along the Cancellaria on your left to the new Corso. If I remember rightly, the tram stops at the Cancellaria—somewhere near it anyway—you will see the sign. But be sure to take the tram marked San Pietro, *stazione* Termini, No. 1.”

===Chinese===
「去的。车身上写着『圣彼得—特米尼车站（stazione Termini）』的那些就去。」她向海尔格解释道。

「哦，那趟呀！它先要绕过卡波莱卡塞（Capo le Case）和卢多维西（Ludovisi），兜上好大一个圈子——坐那趟车去车站，起码得一个钟头。」

「不，亲爱的，那是直达的——一直沿民族街（Via Nazionale）走。」

「才不是呢，」另一位执拗地说，「它先得拐到拉特兰（Lateran）去。」

高个儿姑娘转向海尔格：「第一个路口向右拐，你会走进一处像是市场的地方。从那儿沿着左手边的坎切拉里亚（Cancellaria）走，就到了新的科尔索大街。如果我没记错，电车在坎切拉里亚有一站——反正就在那附近——你会看见站牌的。不过千万记住，要坐标着『圣彼得—特米尼（stazione Termini）车站』的1路车。」

===Original===
Helge stood somewhat crestfallen, listening to the foreign names which the girls used with such easy familiarity, and, shaking his head, said: “I am afraid I shall never be able to find it—perhaps I had better walk till I find a cab.”

“We might go with you to the stop,” said the tall one.

The little one whispered peevishly something in Italian, but the other answered her decisively. Helge felt still more confused at these asides, which he did not understand.

“Thank you, but please do not trouble. I am sure to find my way home somehow or other.”

“It is no trouble,” said the tall one, starting to walk; “it is on our way.”

===Chinese===
海尔格有些垂头丧气地站着，听两位姑娘把这一连串外国名字说得这样娴熟随意，摇了摇头说：「恐怕我怎么也找不着——也许我还是走着去好，走到碰上一辆马车为止。」

「我们可以陪你走到电车站。」高个儿的那位说。

小个子姑娘用意大利语气鼓鼓地嘀咕了句什么，另一位却斩钉截铁地回了一句。这些他听不懂的旁白，让海尔格愈发摸不着头脑。

「谢谢，可请别麻烦了。我总归能想法子找到回去的路。」

「一点也不麻烦，」高个儿姑娘说着抬脚走了，「正顺路呢。」

===Original===
“It is very kind of you; I suppose it is rather difficult to find one’s way about in Rome, is it not?” he said, by way of conversation—“especially when it is dark.”

“Oh no, you will soon get into it.”

“I only arrived here today. I came from Florence this morning by train.” The smaller one said something in an undertone in Italian. The tall one asked: “Was it very cold in Florence?”

“Yes, bitterly cold. It is milder here, is it not? I wrote my mother anyway yesterday to send my winter coat.”

“Well, it is cold enough here too sometimes. Did you like Florence? How long were you there?”

===Chinese===
「你们太好了。我想，在罗马认路怕是挺不容易的吧？」他找话说——「尤其是天黑的时候。」

「哦，不，你很快就会上手的。」

「我今天才到。是今天早上坐火车从佛罗伦萨来的。」小个子姑娘又用意大利语低声说了句什么。高个儿姑娘问：「佛罗伦萨很冷吗？」

「冷，冷得刺骨。这儿暖和些，是不是？反正我昨天已经写信给母亲，叫她把我的冬大衣寄来。」

「唉，这儿有时候也够冷的。你喜欢佛罗伦萨吗？在那儿待了多久？」

===Original===
“A fortnight. I think I shall like Rome better than Florence.”

The other young girl smiled—she had been muttering to herself in Italian all the time—but the tall one went on in her pleasant, quiet voice:

“I don’t believe there is *any* town one could love as much as Rome.”

“Is your friend Italian?” asked Helge.

“No; Miss Jahrman is Norwegian. We speak Italian because I want to learn, and she is very good at it. My name is Winge,” she added. “That is the Cancellaria.” She pointed towards a big, dark palace.

===Chinese===
「两个星期。我想我会更喜欢罗马，胜过佛罗伦萨。」

另一位姑娘笑了——她一直在用意大利语自言自语——高个儿姑娘却用她那悦耳安详的嗓音接着说：

「我不相信天底下有『任何』一座城，能叫人爱得像罗马这样。」

「你的朋友是意大利人吗？」海尔格问。

「不是，雅尔曼（Jahrman）小姐是挪威人。我们讲意大利语，是因为我想学，而她讲得非常好。我叫温格（Winge）。」她又补了一句，指向一座黑黢黢的大宫殿：「那就是坎切拉里亚。」

===Original===
“Is the courtyard as fine as it is reported to be?”

“Yes; it is very fine. I will show you which car.” While they stood waiting two men came across the street.

“Hullo, you here!” exclaimed one of them.

“Good evening,” said the other. “What luck! We can go together. Have you been to look at the corals?”

“It was closed,” said Miss Jahrman sulkily.

===Chinese===
「那院子真像人们说的那么漂亮吗？」

「是的，非常漂亮。我会指给你看坐哪趟车。」他们站着等车的当儿，两个男子穿过街朝他们走来。

「嘿，你们在这儿呢！」其中一个喊道。

「晚上好。」另一位说。「真巧！咱们可以同行了。你们去看珊瑚了吗？」

「关门了。」雅尔曼小姐悻悻地说。

===Original===
“We have met a fellow-countryman, and promised to show him the right tram,” Miss Winge explained, introducing: “Mr. Gram—Mr. Heggen, artist, and Mr. Ahlin, sculptor.”

“I don’t know if you remember me, Mr. Heggen—my name is Gram; we met three years ago on the Mysusaeter.”

“Oh yes—certainly. And so you are in Rome?”

Ahlin and Miss Jahrman had stood talking to one another in whispers. The girl came up to her friend and said: “I am going home, Jenny. I am not in the mood for Frascati tonight.”

“But, my dear, you suggested it yourself.”

===Chinese===
「我们遇上了一位同胞，答应送他上该坐的电车。」温格小姐解释道，又引见说：「格拉姆先生——这位是赫根（Heggen）先生，画家；这位是阿林（Ahlin）先生，雕塑家。」

「不知道您还记不记得我，赫根先生——我叫格拉姆；三年前我们在缪苏塞特（Mysusaeter）山间避暑时见过面。」

「哦，记得——当然记得。这么说，你到罗马来了？」

阿林和雅尔曼小姐刚才一直站在一旁窃窃私语。这时那姑娘走到她朋友跟前说：「我要回家了，珍妮（Jenny）。今晚我没兴致去弗拉斯卡蒂（Frascati）。」

「可是，亲爱的，是你自己提议的呀。」

===Original===
“Well, not Frascati anyway—ugh! sit there and mope with thirty old Danish ladies of every possible age and sex.”

“We can go somewhere else. But there is your tram coming, Mr. Gram.”

“A thousand thanks for your help. Shall I see you again—at the Scandinavian club, perhaps?”

The tram stopped in front of them. Miss Winge said: “I don’t know—perhaps you would like to come with us now; we were going to have a glass of wine somewhere, and hear some music.”

“Thank you.” Helge hesitated, looking round at the others a little embarrassed. “I should be very pleased, but”—and, turning with confidence to Miss Winge of the fair face and the kind voice, he said, with an awkward smile, “you all know one another—perhaps you would rather not have a stranger with you?”

===Chinese===
「反正弗拉斯卡蒂是不去的——呃！跟三十个丹麦老太太坐在那儿干闷着，什么年纪的、什么性别的全都有。」

「我们可以去别的地方。不过你的电车来了，格拉姆先生。」

「多谢你们帮忙。以后还能再见吗——也许，在斯堪的纳维亚俱乐部？」

电车在他们面前停了下来。温格小姐说：「说不好——要不你现在就同我们一起去吧；我们正打算找个地方喝杯酒，听听音乐。」

「谢谢。」海尔格迟疑着，有点局促地朝其余几位望了望。「我是非常乐意的，只是」——他满心信赖地转向那位容颜明净、嗓音和善的温格小姐，带着一丝笨拙的微笑说——「你们彼此都相识——或许，你们宁可不带一个生人吧？」

===Original===
“Indeed no,” she said, smiling—“it would be very nice—and there—your tram’s gone now. You know Heggen already, and now you know us. We’ll see you get home all right, so if you are not tired, let us go.”

“Tired, not a bit. I should love to come,” said Helge eagerly.

The other three began to propose different cafés. Helge knew none of the names; his father had not mentioned them. Miss Jahrman rejected them all.

“Very well, then, let us go down to St. Agostino; you know the one, Gunnar, where they give you that first-rate claret,” and Jenny began to walk on, accompanied by Heggen.

“There is no music,” retorted Miss Jahrman.

===Chinese===
「当然不会。」她微笑着说——「有你会很愉快的——你瞧，你的电车这就开走了。你已经认识赫根了，这会儿又认识了我们。我们会看着你好好到家的。所以，只要你不累，就走吧。」

「累？一点儿也不。我巴不得一起去呢。」海尔格急切地说。

另外三个人开始各荐一家家咖啡馆。这些名字海尔格一个也没听说过；他父亲不曾提起过。雅尔曼小姐把它们一家家都否了。

「那好，我们就去圣阿戈斯蒂诺（St. Agostino）吧；你知道那家儿的，贡纳尔（Gunnar），他们那儿的那种头等好的红葡萄酒。」珍妮说着便动身往前走，赫根陪在她身边。

「那儿没有音乐。」雅尔曼小姐回了一句。

===Original===
“Oh yes, the man with a squint and the other fellow are there almost every night. Don’t let us waste time.”

Helge followed with Miss Jahrman and the Swedish sculptor.

“Have you been long in Rome, Mr. Gram?”

“No, I came this morning from Florence.”

Miss Jahrman laughed. Helge felt rather snubbed. He ought perhaps to have said he was tired, and gone home. On their way down through dark, narrow streets Miss Jahrman talked all the time to the sculptor, and scarcely answered when he tried to speak to her. But before he had made up his mind he saw the other couple vanish through a narrow door down the street.

===Chinese===
「有的，那个斜眼的和一个家伙，几乎每晚都在。咱们别耽搁工夫了。」

海尔格跟在后面，同雅尔曼小姐和那位瑞典雕塑家走在一起。

「您来罗马很久了吗，格拉姆先生？」

「不久，我是今天早上从佛罗伦萨来的。」

雅尔曼小姐笑了一声。海尔格觉得讨了个没趣。也许他本该说自己累了，先回家去的。一路穿行在昏暗狭窄的街巷里，雅尔曼小姐自始至终只顾同那位雕塑家说话，他想要搭话，她几乎不答理。可不等他拿定主意，他已望见前头那一对儿穿过街尾一道窄门，倏地不见了。

### II / 二

===Original===
“What’s wrong with Cesca again tonight? We are getting too much of her tempers lately. Take off your coat, Jenny, or you’ll be cold when you go out.” Heggen hung his coat and hat on a peg and sat down on a rush chair.

“She is not well, poor girl, and that man Gram, you see, followed us a while before he dared to speak to us; and anything of that kind always puts her out of temper; she has a weak heart, you know.”

“Sorry for her. The cheek of the man.”

“Poor thing, he was wandering listlessly about and could not find his way home. He doesn’t seem used to travelling. Did you know him before?”

“Haven’t the slightest recollection of it. I may have met him somewhere. Here they are.”

Ahlin took Miss Jahrman’s coat.

===Chinese===
「切斯卡（Cesca）今晚又是怎么了？近来她这脾气，咱们领教得也够多了。把外衣脱了吧，珍妮（Jenny），不然等会儿出去要着凉的。」赫根（Heggen）把大衣和帽子挂上衣钩，在一把灯心草垫的椅子上坐了下来。

「她身子不舒服，可怜的姑娘。还有那个姓格拉姆（Gram）的男人，你瞧，跟了我们好一段路才敢开口搭话；凡是这一类的事，总是惹得她心烦意乱。她心脏不好，你是知道的。」

「可怜的人。那男人脸皮倒真厚。」

「可怜的家伙，他没精打采地四处乱转，连回客栈的路都找不着。看样子他不习惯出门。你从前认识他吗？」

「一点儿也记不起来了。兴许在哪儿见过一面吧。他们来了。」

阿林（Ahlin）接过雅尔曼（Jahrman）小姐的外衣。

===Original===
“By Jove!” said Heggen. “How smart you are tonight, Cesca. Pretty as paint.”

She smiled, evidently pleased, and smoothed her hips; then, taking Heggen by the shoulders: “Move out, please, I want to sit by Jenny.”

How pretty she is, thought Helge. Her dress was a brilliant green, the skirt so high-waisted that the rounded breasts rose as out of a cup. There was a golden sheen in the folds of the velvet, and the bodice was cut low round the pale, full throat. She was very dark; small, jet-black curls fell from under the brown bell-shaped hat about her soft, rosy cheeks. The face was that of a little girl, with full, round lids over deep, brown eyes, and charming dimples about the small, red mouth.

Miss Winge too was good-looking, but could not compete with her friend. She was as fair as the other was dark; her blonde hair brushed back from a high, white forehead had tints of flaming gold in it; her skin was a delicate pink and white. Even the brows and lashes round her steel-grey eyes were a fair, golden brown. The mouth was too big for her face, with its short, straight nose and blue-veined temples, and the lips were pale, but when she smiled, she showed even, pearly teeth. Her figure was slender: the long, slim neck, the arms covered with a fair, silken down, and the long, thin hands. She was tall, and so slim that she was almost like an overgrown boy. She seemed very young. She had a narrow, white turned-down collar round the V-shaped neck of her dress and revers of the same kind round her short sleeves. Her dress of soft, pale grey silk was gathered round the waist and on the shoulders—obviously to make her look less thin. She wore a row of pink beads round her neck, which were reflected in rosy spots on her skin.

Helge Gram sat down quietly at the end of the table and listened to the others talking about a friend of theirs who had been ill. An old Italian, with a dirty white apron covering his broad waistcoat, came up to ask what they required.

===Chinese===
「好家伙！」赫根说。「切斯卡，你今晚可真俏丽，美得跟画儿似的。」

她笑了，分明很受用，抚了抚胯上的衣裙；随即抓住赫根的双肩：「劳驾往边上让让，我要挨着珍妮坐。」

她多美啊，海尔格（Helge）心想。她那条裙子是娇艳的绿色，裙腰提得那么高，圆润的双乳像从一只杯子里涌出来似的。丝绒的褶皱间泛着金色的光泽，领口开得很低，裸露出白皙丰腴的喉颈。她的肤色很深；棕色钟形帽的帽檐下钻出一个个乌黑的小卷儿，垂落在她柔嫩红润的脸颊上。那张脸活脱是个小姑娘的脸，深棕色的眼睛上覆着圆圆的、饱满的眼睑，小巧红润的嘴旁现出迷人的酒窝。

温格（Winge）小姐也是好看的，只是同她这位朋友一比就要逊色了。她的白皙正像对方的深色；淡金色的头发从高而白净的额头向后梳拢，泛出火焰般的金红；皮肤是娇嫩的粉白。连她钢灰色眼睛四周的眉毛和睫毛，也是浅淡的金褐色。那张嘴配她的脸嫌大了些——脸上一副短而端正的鼻子，太阳穴上隐现青色的血管——唇色很淡，可她嫣然一笑，便露出珍珠般齐整的牙齿。她身条纤瘦：脖颈细长，手臂上覆着一层浅金色的柔毛，一双手也是瘦长的。她个子高挑，瘦得几乎像个抽条过快的半大男孩。她看上去非常年轻。V形领口的连衣裙上围着一条窄窄的白色翻领，短短的袖口也镶着一圈同样的翻边。她那身柔软的浅灰色绸裙在腰间和肩头都收了褶——明摆着是想让她显得不那么单薄。她颈间挂着一串粉色珠子，映得肌肤上星星点点地泛出玫瑰色。

海尔格·格拉姆（Helge Gram）在桌子尽头安安静静地坐下，听旁人谈起他们一位生病的朋友。一个年老的意大利人走过来问他们要些什么，宽大的马甲外面罩着一条脏兮兮的白围裙。

===Original===
“Red or white, sweet or dry, what do you like, Gram?” said Heggen, turning to him.

“Mr. Gram must have half a litre of my claret,” said Jenny Winge. “It is one of the best things you can have in Rome, and that is no small praise, you know.”

The sculptor pushed his cigarette-case over to the ladies. Miss Jahrman took one and lighted it.

“No, Cesca—don’t!” begged Miss Winge.

===Chinese===
「红的还是白的，甜的还是干的，你爱哪样，格拉姆？」赫根转身问他。

「格拉姆先生得喝半升我这种红葡萄酒，」珍妮·温格（Jenny Winge）说。「这是在罗马（Rome）能喝到的东西里数一数二的了——您知道，这可不是一句小话。」

雕塑家把烟盒推到几位女士面前。雅尔曼小姐拈出一支，点上了。

「别抽，切斯卡——求求你！」温格小姐央求道。

===Original===
“Yes,” said Miss Jahrman. “I shan’t be any better if I don’t smoke, and I am cross tonight.”

“Why are you cross?” asked Ahlin.

“Because I did not get those corals.”

“Were you going to wear them tonight?” asked Heggen.

“No, but I had made up my mind to have them.”

“I see,” said Heggen, laughing, “and tomorrow you will decide to have the malachite necklace.”

===Chinese===
「就要抽，」雅尔曼小姐说。「不抽我也好不到哪儿去，何况我今晚心里正烦着呢。」

「你为什么烦？」阿林问。

「因为我没买到那串珊瑚。」

「你今晚原打算戴它吗？」赫根问。

「不是，可我早已拿定主意，非把它弄到手不可。」

「我明白了，」赫根笑道，「明天你就会拿定主意要那串孔雀石项链了。」

===Original===
“No, I won’t, but it is awfully annoying. Jenny and I rushed down on purpose because of those wretched corals.”

“But you had the good luck to meet us, otherwise you would have been obliged to go to Frascati, to which you seem to have taken a sudden dislike.”

“I would not have gone to Frascati, you may be sure of that, Gunnar, and it would have been much better for me, because now that you have made me come I want to smoke and drink and be out the whole night.”

“I was under the impression that you had suggested it yourself.”

“I think the malachite necklace was very fine,” said Ahlin, by way of interrupting—“and very cheap.”

“Yes, but in Florence malachite is much cheaper still. This thing cost forty-seven lire. In Florence, where Jenny bought her *cristallo rosso*, I could have got one for thirty-five. Jenny gave only eighteen for hers. But I will make him give me the corals for ninety lire.”

===Chinese===
「不，我才不会呢。可这事情实在气人。我和珍妮专为那串倒霉珊瑚跑了一趟。」

「可你们运气好，撞上了我们，要不然就只好上弗拉斯卡蒂（Frascati）去了——你们对那地方好像突然讨厌起来。」

「我才不会去弗拉斯卡蒂呢，这你尽管放心，贡纳尔（Gunnar），再说不去反倒好——如今既然是你们把我拽来的，我就只想抽烟、喝酒、在外头逛个通宵。」

「我倒一直以为是你们自己提议的呢。」

「我看那串孔雀石项链确实精美，」阿林插进来打岔道——「而且便宜。」

「精美是精美，可在佛罗伦萨（Florence），孔雀石还便宜得多。这串就花了四十七里拉。在佛罗伦萨——珍妮就是在那儿买她那条 *cristallo rosso*（红水晶）的——我花三十五里拉就能拿下一串。珍妮买她那条只花了十八。不过，我自有办法叫他九十里拉把那串珊瑚让给我。」

===Original===
“I don’t quite understand your economy,” said Heggen.

“I don’t want to talk about it any more,” said Miss Jahrman. “I am sick of all this talk—and tomorrow I am going to buy the corals.”

“But isn’t ninety lire an awful price for corals?” Heggen risked the question.

“They are not ordinary corals, you know,” Miss Jahrman deigned to answer. “They are *contadina* corals, a fat chain with a gold clasp and heavy drops—like that.”

“*Contadina*—is that a special kind of coral?” asked Helge.

===Chinese===
「你这套理财经，我真弄不明白。」赫根说。

「这事我不想再提了，」雅尔曼小姐说。「这套话我听腻了——反正明天我就去把珊瑚买下来。」

「可九十里拉买串珊瑚，价钱不贵得吓人吗？」赫根冒昧问了一句。

「那可不是寻常的珊瑚，你知道的，」雅尔曼小姐屈尊答道。「那是『农家女』（*contadina*）戴的珊瑚，链子粗实，金搭扣，坠子沉甸甸的——就像这么大小。」

「『农家女』——是珊瑚里的一个特别品种吗？」海尔格问。

===Original===
“No. It is what the *contadinas* wear.”

“But I don’t know what a *contadina* is, you see.”

“A peasant girl. Have you not seen those big, dark red, polished corals they wear? Mine are exactly the colour of raw beef, and the bead in the middle is as big as that”—and she formed a ring with her thumb and forefinger the size of an egg.

“How beautiful they must be,” said Helge, pleased to get hold of the thread of conversation. “I don’t know what malachite is, or *cristallo rossa*, but I am sure that corals like those would suit you better than anything.”

“Do you hear, Ahlin? And you wanted me to have the malachite necklace. Heggen’s scarf-pin is malachite—take it off, Gunnar—and Jenny’s beads are *cristallo rosso*, not *rossa*—red rock crystals, you know.”

===Chinese===
「不是。就是『农家女』们戴的那种。」

「可您瞧，我压根儿不知道『农家女』是什么呀。」

「就是农家姑娘。你没见过她们戴的那种又大又黑红、磨得锃亮的珊瑚吗？我那串的颜色跟生牛肉一模一样，正中间那颗珠子有这么大」——说着她用拇指和食指圈出一个鸡蛋大小的圈儿。

「那一定好看极了，」海尔格说，总算攀上了话头，心里很高兴。「孔雀石是什么，*cristallo rossa* 又是什么，我都不知道，可我敢说，那样的珊瑚比什么都更配您。」

「听见没有，阿林？你还撺掇我买孔雀石项链。赫根的领带别针就是孔雀石的——取下来瞧瞧，贡纳尔——还有珍妮这串珠子是 *cristallo rosso*，不是 *rossa*——就是红水晶嘛，你不知道吧。」

===Original===
She handed him the scarf-pin and the necklace. The beads were warm from contact with the young girl’s neck. He looked at them a while; in every bead there were small flaws, as it were, which absorbed the light.

“You ought really to wear corals, Miss Jahrman. You would look exactly like a Roman *contadina* yourself.”

“You don’t say so!” She smiled, pleased. “Do you hear, you others?”

“You have an Italian name, too,” said Helge eagerly.

“No. I was named after my grandmother, but the Italian family I lived with last year could not pronounce my ugly name, and since then I have stuck to the Italian version of it.”

===Chinese===
她把别针和珠串递给他。珠子上还留着少女脖颈的温热。他端详了半晌；每一颗珠子里仿佛都有细小的瑕疵，把光都吸了进去。

「您其实真该戴珊瑚，雅尔曼小姐。戴上它，您看上去准会活脱脱像个罗马的『农家女』。」

「是吗！」她笑了，很是得意。「你们都听见了没有？」

「您还有个意大利名字呢。」海尔格热切地说。

「才没有呢。我的名字是照着外祖母取的，可去年我住的那户意大利人家念不准我这个难听的名字，打那以后，我就一直用它意大利化的拼法。」

===Original===
“Francesca,” said Ahlin, in a whisper.

“I shall always think of you as Francesca—*signorina Francesca*.”

“Why not Miss Jahrman? Unfortunately we cannot speak Italian together, since you don’t know the language.” She turned to the others. “Jenny, Gunnar—I am going to buy the corals tomorrow.”

“Yes; I think I heard you say so,” said Heggen.

“And I will *not* pay more than ninety.”

===Chinese===
「弗朗切斯卡（Francesca），」阿林悄声道。

「往后我要一直把您当作弗朗切斯卡——*signorina Francesca*（弗朗切斯卡小姐）。」

「干吗不叫雅尔曼小姐？可惜咱们没法用意大利语交谈，谁让您不懂这门语言呢。」她转向旁人。「珍妮，贡纳尔——我明天要去买那串珊瑚。」

「是；这话我好像已经听你说过一遍了。」赫根说。

「而且我『决不』付比九十更高的价。」

===Original===
“You always have to bargain here,” said Helge, as one who knows. “I went into a shop this afternoon near St. Pietro and bought this thing for my mother. They asked seven lire, but I got it for four. Don’t you think it was cheap?” He put the thing on the table.

Francesca looked at it with contempt. “It costs two fifty in the market. I took a pair of them to each of the maids at home last year.”

“The man said it was old,” retorted Helge.

“They always do, when they see that people don’t understand, and don’t know the language.”

“You don’t think it is pretty?” said Helge, downcast, and wrapped the pink tissue paper round his treasure. “Don’t you think I can give it to my mother?”

===Chinese===
「在这儿买东西总得讲价钱，」海尔格以行家的口吻说。「今天下午我在圣彼得（San Pietro）附近一家铺子里给我母亲买了这个小玩意儿。他们开口要七里拉，让我四里拉讲到手了。你们说便宜不便宜？」他把那东西摆到桌上。

弗朗切斯卡轻蔑地瞥了它一眼。「市场上两里拉半一个。去年我带回去一打，家里的女仆每人送了一对。」

「掌柜的还说这是老物件。」海尔格回了一句。

「他们瞧见人家不懂行、又不懂话，就总来这一套。」

「你觉得它不好看吗？」海尔格垂头丧气，拿粉色的薄棉纸把他的宝贝重新包起来。「你们当真觉得我不能把它送给我母亲吗？」

===Original===
“I think it is hideous,” said Francesca, “but, of course, I don’t know your mother’s taste.”

“What on earth shall I do with it, then?” sighed Helge.

“Give it to your mother,” said Jenny. “She will be pleased that you have remembered her. Besides, people at home like those things. We who live out here see so much that we become more critical.”

Francesca reached her hand for Ahlin’s cigarette-case, but he did not want to let her have it; they whispered together eagerly, then she flung it away, calling: “Giuseppe!”

Helge understood that she ordered the man to bring her some cigarettes. Ahlin got up suddenly: “My dear Miss Jahrman—I meant only to … you know it is not good for you to smoke so much.”

===Chinese===
「我觉得它难看死了，」弗朗切斯卡说，「不过当然喽，我不了解你母亲的口味。」

「那我拿它究竟怎么办呢？」海尔格叹道。

「送给你母亲，」珍妮说。「她见你还惦记着她，会高兴的。再说，家乡的人都喜欢这类玩意儿。我们这些住在外头的人见得太多，眼光就变得挑剔了。」

弗朗切斯卡伸手去够阿林的烟盒，他却不肯给她；两人急促地低声咕哝了几句，随后她把烟盒一甩，喊道：「朱塞佩（Giuseppe）！」

海尔格明白了，她这是吩咐侍者给她拿烟。阿林霍地站起来：「亲爱的雅尔曼小姐——我本意只是想……您知道的，烟抽得这么凶，对您没有好处。」

===Original===
Francesca rose. She had tears in her eyes.

“Never mind. I want to go home.”

“Miss Jahrman—Cesca.” Ahlin stood holding her cloak and begged her quietly not to go. She pressed her handkerchief to her eyes.
